The not asking is entered for the ninth time and there is no reason on the line.** The documents about each other are six and the sixth is the fair copy of the three rules. **He has a dated offer from a Crown office in a sealed envelope and she does not know, and that is a fourth concealment and it is not in the list of three that the state files carried, and it is now on the page in Chapter 110's notebook and in Chapter 106's last paragraph.**
- **The order of an ordinary morning, and the spent sentence. Thread 83, items 236, 247, 248, and item 282.** Spent in a yard in Volume 02 and not re-said in these ten chapters. **Tovan has now added a fifth line to his own week-book, with an hour against it and no figure, about the date of his brother's crossing out, and has entered that he is not going to tell him and that nobody in this county is ever going to ask to see that page. Aren does not know this.** **Aren has a list of six people he has told nothing to, and his brother is one of the six, and the concealment on Aren's side is the offer, the leaf, and the row of files.** The two are separate and both stand. The yard is the only relationship in the book currently getting better and it is getting better by taking four hundred miles of river work and refusing to let a boy of fifteen go.
- **The second severance. Thread 86, item 243.** **The join is not stated once in ten chapters and the marshal's name is not written.** A fourth column of an index of outgoing writs is still ruled and empty and is in a room four hundred miles from the man who is now the middle of two forms. **Volume 04 is where it opens. It opens in Chapter 894.**
- **A figure about a county with no name in it, and an office that can now be served. Thread 84, items 240, 241, and item 286.** **The Marches' paper has been spent on the back of it in four minutes on a Sunday by a construction that is the same one that spent the count of the ninth of Greenmonth, and the magistrate who spent it is frightened of her own reasoning and has written the fear on the back of a paper where nobody will find it.** **The second proposed count — of oath-bound persons in a border county, returnable on a named day, with a space for a figure and no space for a name — is Chapter 113 and is answered on the morning he goes out, and no name is written in a Crown's hand for the second time.** A records office that can be sued is about to be asked to produce a person.
- **A man of fifty-eight who does not know which kiln he fires. Thread 74, item 252.** **Not mentioned in Chapters 101–110, not staged, and the eighth month is now behind him.** The Marches' own letter to that county is the volume's next-volume question and is not answered inside Volume 03, and the address is a line and two hands and a witness at each end and the witness at that end was a person the county chose and who was struck.
- **The eighth county cell, the four ruled cells, the sixth line. Thread 93, item 266.** **The eighth ruled line in the served schedule is not looked at in this volume and no character refers to it.** The city is still four thousand and the seven villages seven hundred and twenty and the two have not been added and are not going to be. **The leaf of a return in Mara's press has two ruled cells at the foot with nothing in them and not four, so that Chapter 79's blank stays unique, and no two of the five blanks are in one room, in one chapter or in one sentence, and the fifth space with no sixth and the empty county cell in the ninth line of the index are not touched in these ten chapters.** The brine weights are not mentioned, not filed, and not exhibited.
- **The tile on the coping, and the half tile, and the tile in a press. Thread 92, items 237, 251, 252, and item 284.** **Not touched, not looked at, not explained, and the device is named by nobody.** It is named once in this block and only as *a coping on a river this county does not have*, on the thirtieth of Longlight, **and on that day nothing was struck on it at all, because a boy of sixteen rang a bell on the wrong day to find out whether the far end would hear, and the far end keeps Mondays and did not.** No Volume 03 batch may schedule an explanation, a flashback, an origin, a maker or a deathbed. It is now also on the far side of a road that closes in Block 4.
- **The marginal instruction and the officer who wrote the letters. Thread 85, item 260, and items 274, 275, 278.** **Untouched as a legal explanation and now three things at once.** It is printed on the back of every form; it is quoted verbatim in Chapters 102 and 105 as Chapter 51 has it; **it is cited in a file, in the office's own marginal note of nine words, *Fourth column relied upon as the office of record for the object. Return forwarded. No further returnable date*, which is the practice relied on for the first time in nineteen years and which the office itself does not know is a practice.** **Its worked example is a real man and a real guild and is the reason a delegation came four hundred miles, and a later writer may not have anybody explain the clause in a sentence and Sabin Dain may not be the person who explains it and may not change her want.** Dain's offer is made in writing in Chapter 105, is refused in writing in Chapters 148–150, and the recruitment is revealed by her, in a document, on purpose, and she says in that document that it is not going to work and that she has not said so to her own office.
- **The two unserved books, and the half tile, and the body corporate's door. Item 262.** All three are in the room in Chapter 106 and in Chapter 109 and none of them explains anything. **The body corporate is the reason the magistrate is afraid and the reason the Marches' paper could be spent, and both of those facts are on the page and neither of them is joined to the other on the page.**
- **The banned sums, the two Hushwater figures, and the money rule.** **Four thousand and seven hundred and twenty were never added and no Crown's figure and no served schedule figure is in this batch at all. Hushwater Reach's near sixty and Hushwater's eighty are not in this batch. No Ash figure is on the page in this batch and the 456 is not on the page in any form. Every money column in this batch is added in pence and footed on the page before the total is said aloud**, and the Chapter 97 bill and the Chapter 10 column are untouched and not re-derived and nothing is scaled out of them.
- **The Stage 3 decision. Item 257.** **Stage 3 is not reached in Volume 03 and the cut is not named, refused, dated, priced or described by anybody as a thing he could do if he chose.** The guild's fourth term in Chapter 104 is a trade rule about ditches and is not the cut. The climax of the volume remains a man standing on a structure and the permanent limp is the only new bodily cost.
- **The three known arithmetic defects, recorded so that no later phase treats them as fresh.** Item 263, the promise's age, a month out in the prose and in five state files, with the shape canon and **no Volume 03 figure derived from it** — and **Batch 0011 used the shape and not the number: the torn sheet of Chapter 92 is dated the fifth of Greenmonth in Chapter 106 and the twenty-third the review pass found in the first draft and removed.** Item 264, Chapter 70's eight weeks, which stands as a known defect. Item 265, Petch's dates, where the chapter's are canon. Item 267, the standing rule, which **Batch 0011 obeyed and which produced nineteen figures introduced in one chapter and re-derived in another, of which the repair pass caught nine; a later phase states a figure, checks it, and records the check, and takes the fact from the page and not from a state file.**

**What Batch 0012 inherits as six questions and two answers.** **A Marches delegation has been, and has gone home on the eighteenth on a calendar of its own, and there is no instrument in this kingdom that will tell this county whether it got there. A guild of nineteen men and a first hand who gives terms before he is asked is four hundred miles off and has not been asked whether it will hold a road. A woman of thirty-eight has eight thousand and thirty-one lines in a book nobody has ever asked to see. A seed-carter's whole name is on the back of a leaf in a press in Merefen and the press cannot be served and the man cannot be reached. A road warden's rank and a sealed record of himself are in an envelope two hundred miles off and unanswered. And an instrument of the ninth of Longlight of last year, in force, barring the assembly of any body of this county in respect of an object that is not named on it, is the standing legal weather of the whole of the next block.**

## Threads Batch 0012 carries into Batch 0013

**Written by the Volume 03 Batch 0012 phase, cross-referenced to `outline/volume-03.md` §8 Block 3 and to continuity items 288 onward. No thread below is closed. Threads 82–95 all stand exactly as the Volume 02 close and Batch 0011 record them and none of them is touched in Chapters 111–120 except where a chapter says on the page that a thing happened.**

- **The breach. Thread 82, items 238 and 263, item 283, item 303.** Open. **Not repaired, not to be repaired in any scene, aside, document or apology in Volume 03, and Volume 04's chosen intimacy is bought with it.** What Batch 0012 added and what may not be undone: **the count of not-askings is still nine, entered on the sixteenth of Longlight, and Chapter 111 has her refuse in advance to enter a tenth about this**, because entering a tenth would be entering a question. He gives her a number and nothing else and tells her in the same breath a fact that is not a question. **She has now withdrawn an entry of her own fair copy in her own hand, with the date and the hour, on the ground that it was wrong, and the entry beside it says that this office has been wrong in the same direction three times this year and has no procedure for it** — which is the third rule doing its work and not a cure, on the page, in a document. The three rules are untouched. **A fourth concealment is in writing and in a bag: the reply to a Crown office that neither accepts nor refuses, of which nobody in Merefen knows.** The tile is not mentioned to her. No apology, no question about the morning, no misunderstanding made available, in any of the ten chapters.
- **The order of an ordinary morning, and the spent sentence. Thread 83, items 236, 247, 248, item 282.** **Not re-said, not improved on, not alluded to as a thing to be taken back, in any of the ten chapters.** What the block did to the two of them: **eleven words about a length of hemp across a bench-board in nineteen months; Toban's week-book now has a sixth line with an hour and no figure and he has told the man at the drawknife and not his brother and has entered that he is not going to tell him; a reply to a Crown office in a bag and a list of six that has become seven; a peer of the wharf road's remark about the bar through oak, written on a tally-board, in a yard's book, and not said to either of them.** **The yard is the only relationship in the book getting better and it got better by costing six hundred and forty-eight pence and refusing to let a boy of fifteen go.** Each brother still has a page the other does not know about, and the block did not close either.
- **The second severance. Thread 86, item 243.** **The join is not stated once in these ten chapters and the marshal's name is not written.** A fourth column of an index of outgoing writs is still ruled and empty. **Volume 04 is where it opens, in Chapter 894.** Untouched.
- **A figure about a county with no name in it, and an office that can now be served. Thread 84, items 240, 241, item 286, item 299.** **The second proposed count was spent on the morning of the twenty-second with five hundred and ninety-seven, a figure about her own office, footed twice in two orders, and the construction took four minutes having taken nine the first time.** **The office's reply arrived with the request, in a woman's hand, dated the day before it was asked for, and returned nine thousand two hundred and sixty for a district of eleven counties, in which one county that has returned nothing in eleven years is returned at the average of its neighbours, and it is not an instrument and cannot be refused.** **No name in a Crown's hand for the second time, and there has not been one in this county since the twenty-seventh of Fallowmonth.** The 1,609 of Chapter 93 is a different figure, is not to be disputed, and is not on the page in this block. **A body corporate that can be sued is now on the same page as a figure that cannot be refused, and neither fact is joined to the other on the page.**
- **A man of fifty-eight who does not know which kiln he fires. Thread 74, item 252.** **Not mentioned, not staged and not asked about in Chapters 111–120, and the ninth month is now behind him.** The Marches' own letter to that county is the volume's next-volume question and is not answered inside Volume 03.
- **The eighth county cell, the four ruled cells, the sixth line. Thread 93, item 266.** **The eighth ruled line in the served schedule is not looked at in this block and no character refers to it.** **The city is still four thousand and the seven villages are still seven hundred and twenty and they have not been added and are not going to be, and neither figure is on the page in this block.** The leaf of a return in Mara's press has two ruled cells at the foot with nothing in them and not four, and **the block added a new blank and did not join it to any other: a door-book in a county court with no column in it, and eleven entries in four years and a half that are *no name given*, and a gauge book in a hut at a weir with eight thousand and thirty-one lines in it that has never been asked for, and a nine-line gap in it in a different hand.** **No two of the volume's blanks are in one room, in one chapter or in one sentence, and no character says any two of them are the same.** The brine weights are not mentioned, not filed, not exhibited.
- **The tile on the coping, and the half tile, and the tile in a press. Thread 92, items 237, 251, 252, item 284, item 304.** **Not touched, not looked at, not explained, and the device is named by nobody.** It appears once, in Chapter 112, **only as a coping on a river this county does not have**, through a clerk's minute whose load-bearing clause is that *the coping itself is not described in this entry because no person present has asked what is on it and none of them is charged with it.* **The bell went on a Monday and the far end kept, and nothing about that Monday is in a book, and Mara Vey wrote the word *Monday* into her own book against no person because a day of the week is the only part of it that is not a fact about two counties.** **No Volume 03 batch may schedule an explanation, a flashback, an origin, a maker or a deathbed.** It is on the far side of a road that closes in Block 4.
- **The marginal instruction and the officer who wrote the letters. Thread 85, item 260, items 274, 275, 278, item 300.** **Untouched as a legal explanation, and not explained in a single sentence by anybody.** **A reply went to the provincial town in Chapter 115 that neither accepts nor refuses the offer, does not use the word *custodian*, and does not explain the clause; it states what the office is buying, which is a form, and a woman with a filing system is not in these ten chapters at all and does not change her want.** **Ferrow's name is now on the foot of a sheet in a fair copy in Merefen, the first name in the case that anybody has put anywhere on purpose, and it sits beside the office's printed worked example, and Mara Vey has entered the join in four lines and nobody has acted on it.** Dain's offer is refused in writing in Chapters 148–150 and is not refused in this block.
- **The two unserved books, and the half tile, and the body corporate's door. Item 262.** All three are in the room in Chapter 111 and none of them explains anything. **The body corporate is the reason a magistrate is frightened and the reason a figure of nine thousand two hundred and sixty cannot be refused, and both facts are on the page and neither is joined to the other on the page.**
- **The banned sums, the two Hushwater figures, and the money rule.** **Four thousand and seven hundred and twenty were never added and no Crown's figure and no served schedule figure is in this block at all. Hushwater Reach's near sixty and Hushwater's eighty are not in this block. No Ash figure is on the page and the 456 is not on the page in any form. Nine money figures were introduced and all nine are footed in pence on the page before the total is said aloud: 102, 108, 597 (twice, in two orders), 2,880, 360, 3,240, 2,592, 288, 648, 352 — and the Chapter 10 column and the Chapter 108 figures are untouched and not re-derived and nothing is scaled out of them.** **The block's new arithmetic to carry: 288 pence is six marks or six shillings a man, and 288 + 360 = 648 is the last fifth the guild's first hand gave up, so the yard's exposure and the man's gift are the same number and not the same money, and the yard's book holds the fact of it and not the paper.**
- **The Stage 3 decision. Item 257.** **Stage 3 is not reached and the cut is not named, refused, dated, priced or described by anybody as a thing he could do if he chose.** The guild's fourth term is a trade rule about ditches and is not the cut. **The block's strongest holding of the line is in Chapter 120's third term: *a day, when that end names one, and the line is opened on it and on no other day in that year, and if that end does not name a day the line does not open, and I will not attempt it and I will not call it anything else.*** The climax of the volume remains a man standing on a structure and the permanent limp is the only new bodily cost.
- **The three known arithmetic defects, recorded so that no later phase treats them as fresh.** Item 263, the promise's age — **Batch 0012 used the shape and not the number, and derived no figure from it.** Item 264, Chapter 70's eight weeks, which stands as a known defect. Item 265, Petch's dates, where the chapter's are canon. Item 267, the standing rule, and **Batch 0012 obeyed it and the review pass caught the drift figure being produced by the forbidden subtraction, which is the single most load-bearing number in the volume, and caught the ninth-of-Embermonth chain, the delegation's length of stay, the age of the second of the six and four money glosses. A later phase states a figure, checks it, and records the check, and takes the fact from the page and not from a state file.**

**What Batch 0013 inherits as six questions and one answer.** **A county that has refused a road and has no word for refusing, and a woman of thirty-eight with a gauge book of eight thousand and thirty-one lines and a nine-line gap in it who has told him the book is hers and would rather be asked than copied.** A bridgewright has seen the mechanism — a hand's width a year for a hundred and forty years — and has entered *seen, not told* and may not say it. About sixty households on a roll and not on an oath, and a woman of about sixty on three hearths who has never been asked what she swore and who has told him exactly what his county will do with her. A woman of fifty-five who will not have the stone lifted and who is right about the history and wrong about the soil, and who has not yet said the obvious thing. Four men of a fen county are already at work on that county's own calendar and have never once been asked what they swore. And in a fen county four hundred miles off a bench of nine that has been asked four times is still saying yes, a yard is short six hundred and forty-eight pence, a reply to a Crown office is in a bag, and a keeper of records has not asked.

## Threads Batch 0013 carries into Batch 0014

Written by the Volume 03 Batch 0013 phase and amended by its review repair pass, cross-referenced to continuity items 307–322, with items 310, 311, 316 and 317 amended in place. No thread below is closed. All Batch 0012 threads stand except where this block moved them.

- **The breach. Thread 82, items 238/263/283/303.** Open, unrepaired, untouched in this block; no Merefen scene, no tenth not-asking, the ninth stands. Batch 0014 inherits it unchanged.
- **The brothers. Thread 83, items 236/247/248/282.** Unhealed; the spent sentence is not re-said or alluded to. The yard's four men are in the county on its own calendar, the best crew in it, working staging, never asked what they swore. Carries forward.
- **The council and the guild. Items 307–308.** The council refused outside standing a second time and took the guild's terms; the mechanism is said by Thessa Wend and he said nothing; the hand's-width leaf and nine readings now lie with the council. Batch 0014 inherits a bench that looks at ground and a guild that measures honestly.
- **The refused working. Item 310, amended.** Named once and refused once in Chapter 124, dated with anchor, community, forged witness, and written down, unexplained. The word stands twice in the block and may not be spent again in 131–140; Block 4's flood and command refusal must not re-derive it. **The one further occurrence of the string in the block is Chapter 127's flood line on the chapel corner, *a line on the stone that nobody had cut*, which is the water's mark and not the working.**
- **The sixty and the not asking, and the four words nobody will write. Items 311–312 and 318.** About sixty on the roll and not on the oath, fifty-eight certain and two doubtful; the question is raised and not decided. **And a man of sixty-eight has told Aren, once, for nothing, that a book which says a man said nothing is a book that says a man agreed, and Aren has decided his four true words go in his own hand or in nobody's, and the minute-man has entered the silence.** Batch 0014 inherits the subject, not the answer, and inherits the silence, and may not have the four words entered anywhere.
- **The gateway, the boy of eleven, and four answers in no book. Item 319. This is the plant Block 5 stands on.** One child asked whether being asked would put his household on a list, and four people told the truth in a gateway, and nothing was written down. **Ferrow's own warning is the load-bearing sentence: in five rollings, when a list does come along, half of four villages will sign whatever is read out to them because the council was right and not because they were deceived.** Batch 0014 may not contradict the four answers, may not improve them, may not have anybody enter them, and may not let the county find out it was wrong about a list before Block 5.
- **The vault, and three marks on the outside of it. Items 313 and 320.** Nine sealed jars of wheat ungrown forty years, a wall with two answers built up, an unnamed keeper of sixty-six who refuses thanks. Her name is unspent and is Batch 0014's one name: Sian Pugh, 66. Batch 0014 must spend it once and must not re-open whether it is spent. **There are now three mason's marks on the outside of that wall, above last year's flood line, put there with her leave and against his better judgement, and she keeps the measure in her head, and nobody has asked about them and no book holds them.** They are the batch's only physical act of record, they must not be copied into anything, and a man with a lantern near the seed room in Block 4 is entitled to find them.
- **The league and the proposal. Items 314–315.** Gace and seventy-two pence; the first proposal not his, an oath that can be broken, entered without opinion. Batch 0014 inherits the proposal on the table and must not give it to Aren or an office.
- **Ash's fourth term and the correction. Item 316, amended.** Offered wrongly and declined; conversion refused in his notebook as the volume's one plant; **and a decision entered under it, the first he has made in this county, that he will carry nothing else up those stairs unless a person asks him by name.** The two-line fair copy's source is now known to him and not to the county, **and the sender and the author are two women and the leaf says which is which: the woman who keeps the road at that end copied it, and the woman of about sixty on three hearths in the clay county, fourth column *wife*, wrote it, and they are not the same person and the Marches' own woman of sixty on three hearths is a third.** Batch 0014 inherits the known-unknown, inherits the withdrawal, and must use the fifth term, not the fourth, for Block 4's instrument. **`scheduled day` is not to be used of anything Chapter 118 has closed.**
- **The withdrawal and the staging at seven. Item 321.** He has entered that he will bring nothing else to that chamber unless asked by name, the drawknife man has taken a sill as evidence and not as a promise, and he is on the staging at seven in the morning. **The withdrawal is from the room and not from the county, and it is not permanent: Block 5's command in Chapters 137–138 is a second instrument, and the council's nine seconds of refusal is what makes it legible.**
- **Money, calendar, device counts, and the batch's length. Item 317, amended.** 192 and 72 pence footed; no banned sums, no Ash figures, no weekday or month names in the ten chapters; zero rules and zero bold lines; no System panel. The column of eleven counties and its finding are untouched and may not be stated before Chapter 141. **The block is 16,315 words after the repair pass and is still 56 per cent of Batch 0012; Batch 0014 is to write full-length scenes and is not to treat this block as the house length.**
- **Tovan in the room in Chapter 145. Item 322. Flagged, not answered, and not this batch's to answer.** The outline's §13 row for Ch 131–140 contradicted §8 Block 4 on who gets the road and is corrected to Coll Ferrow and the guild; §8 Ch 145, §9 and the series outline keep Tovan Kest for Block 5, and he is four hundred miles off. **Batch 0014 inherits the question and must not answer it.** Batch 0015 decides it on the page.

## Threads Batch 0014 carries into Batch 0015

Written by the Volume 03 Batch 0014 phase, cross-referenced to continuity items 323–340 and the Relationship state at the end of Chapter 140. No thread below is closed. All Batch 0013 threads stand except where this block moved them.

- **The breach. Thread 82, items 238/263/283/303.** Open, unrepaired, untouched. No Merefen scene except one undated four-line coda in Chapter 134; **no tenth not-asking is entered, the ninth stands, and no apology and no mistake has been made available to anybody in Chapters 131–140.** Batch 0015 inherits it unchanged, and Ch 148 is its date.
- **The brothers. Thread 83, items 236/247/248/282/322.** Unhealed and untouched; the spent sentence is not re-said, improved, or alluded to as retractable. **Item 322's question — how Tovan Kest is in the room in Chapter 145 — is flagged, is not answered, and may not be answered in a state file, a summary, or an assertion. Three routes are open and all three keep the scene the outline fixes: he travels, which costs rollings the volume has not got; the hand-over is made to a yard he holds in Merefen and the room is a room on this side of the line, which is colder and better; or the crew's own authority carries the guild's and the guild's carries the road, with the brother named in a document and not in the room. The yard's four men are on the council's account, unfootable, and the best crew in the county, and a man of fifty-two has asked to be entered as not having sworn anything and is in the guild's book with a date and no hour, which is the ground Batch 0015 builds the scene on.**
- **The council and the guild. Items 307–308, 324, 325, 331.** The council has refused outside standing a second time, taken the guild's terms, and now refused a command in nine seconds in its own form and accepted a guild's plan written before he arrived. The mechanism is spent (item 323) and the four hundred and forty yards, the eleven yards, the trough, the pond and the hand's width are its fixed figures. Ferrow's line is fixed: the water in a ditch is somebody's job; the man who went into the Water is unnamed; Gace's proposal is the least dangerous thing in the county; and a council that expects a flood is a council with a plan. **Batch 0015 inherits a bench that has been given a document saying it may not command and a guild that has just been handed the causeway and the waterworks in a fair copy on a board.**
- **The refused working. Item 310.** Named once and refused once in Chapter 124 and not re-derived anywhere in Chapters 131–140. **The string `cut` occurs four times in Chapters 131–140 and none of them is the working** (item 340). Batch 0015 may not spend it and may not price it.
- **The sixty and the not asking, and the four words nobody will write. Items 311–312, 318, 338.** About sixty households on the roll and not on the oath, fifty-eight certain and two doubtful, and the question raised and not decided. **A class of persons has now been entered in the county's only book at the asking of a woman on three hearths: that a woman on three hearths who is not on the oath is not a person this bench asks things of, and that the next one will be standing in the rain outside that window and will not know to come in.** The man of sixty-eight's four true words are still in nobody's book by Aren's own decision and may not be entered anywhere in this volume. **A list of households to be helped arrives in Chapters 141–143 and this is the plant it stands on.**
- **The gateway, the boy of eleven, and four answers in no book. Item 319, item 339.** **Ferrow has now said in the ninth rolling that the gateway is still standing, that the answer in it has not been contradicted by anything, and that a county which answers a child in a gateway and is then proved right for seven rollings about a different thing has not become harder to find.** The thing that came instead of the list he warned about was four lines with no name at the top of it and nothing to sign. **Batch 0015 may not contradict the four answers, may not improve them, may not have anybody enter them, and may not let the county learn it was wrong about a list before the list of households to be helped arrives.**
- **The vault, and three marks on the outside of it. Items 313, 320, 329.** Nine sealed jars back on their shelf in a room with a foot of dried mud in it, kept by **Sian Pugh, 66, named once in Chapter 136 in a yard and not spoken again by anybody in that county.** The three marks have been found twice with a lantern, once in a wet week and once in the flood, and read in daylight in the eighth rolling, and they are not in any book and the keeper keeps the measure in her head. **She has decided nothing and has said she will decide in the spring. Volume 03's name budget is seven of seven and twenty-three of twenty-three slots unfilled: no new face may be added to this volume after Chapter 140 (§14).** Batch 0015 carries all three parts of her want, none of which is on the page yet: the seed in nine jars and not in a wall, that she wants to be the last keeper of a seed room and not the first keeper of a seed house, that she does not want the cooperative's thanks, and the difference between a list of persons to be struck and a list of households to be helped in one sentence, not improved on. **Her decision about the three marks is a different thing and is not part of it.**
- **The league and the proposal. Items 314–315, item 337.** Gace's oath that can be broken has been on the table since the third rolling, entered without opinion, and **Ferrow has now said in a doorway that it is the least dangerous thing in this county and that he has been calling it dangerous for four rollings and will take it back in a room and not in a gateway, and has not told Gace.** The consequence in Gace's proposal is a list of households to be helped. **The finding in item 337 has landed on the same ground: the Marches is the sixth of the eight, a claim about a share of a watercourse kept for a hundred and forty years by living men, and the promise and the release are the only door this county has.** Batch 0015 inherits a plan that is ready to be built on by a man who has stopped being frightened of it, and a council that has been told by a guild that the plan is safe.
- **The flooded county. Items 325, 327, 328, 339.** The Water came into the bed it has always had because the guild took a hundred and forty feet of new bank out of the wrong side of it in three days, and the near side of the vault was saved and nothing else, and **nobody died, and the salt came up in the new land, and the four white villages have eleven places under water and nine gone and the newest white has no dark inch on the top of it.** The water table will come down over four years and the first crop off two of the villages will be a failure. The boundaries are to be re-cut by the people on both sides of them in nine days in the standing water at a cost on a board that foots in pence. **The bank above the pond came down on the fourth day of the ninth rolling and buried about forty yards of the old bank and a barrow and nothing else, and the guild's six worked days are done and the guild is out 480 + 704 + 592 pence on three jobs.**
- **Ash's fourth term, the correction, and the notice. Items 316, 333, 334, 335.** The fourth term was offered wrongly in Chapter 130 and declined; the two-line fair copy's source is known to him and not to the county. **The County of Ash is now closed in writing by a four-line notice, itemised, checkable, in a fair hand, sent by the woman who keeps that end for a bench that asked, entered at the fourth hour on a day that county keeps, containing no name, and its fourth line is the second of the county's own terms given back whole, forty-one words, a refusal of him, and correct.** He called the second route once and the answer was the notice. He holds two routes: Bellweather-Living and the Hearth Marches. **The second of the terms may not be re-worded and its forty-one words are referred to and not reprinted (item 334). `scheduled day` remains barred. Dain's offer is not refused in this block and its written refusal is Chapters 148–150.**
- **The withdrawal and the bar. Items 321, 330, 331.** He entered that he would bring nothing else to that chamber unless asked by name; **Ferrow named it aloud in front of eleven people, Munn asked whether it was true, he said it was and said nobody had asked him, and the breaking is in the council's book with the hour against no person.** The command was the old move and it was right and it was refused in nine seconds, and the plan that replaced it was written before he arrived. **A fair copy on a board under the bench says a stranger holds no office in this county and may not be given one by this council. The withdrawal now has nothing to withdraw from, and Block 5's Chapter 145 hand-over is a second instrument in a room where he has already been refused one.**
- **The finding, and the roll. Items 336, 337, 338.** The column came back down the line in the same basket as the notice, costed at 102 pence of a bench's money, addressed to a shelf, and the bench of nine in the clay county answered unprompted: nine stones, none between two fields. **The finding is spent in Chapter 140 and may not be stated before that point in any form, may not be improved on, and may not be attached to any county but the six of the eight. It is entered in one book in one county, against nobody, with the hour, and it is in nobody's book four hundred miles off, and the keeper has refused to put it in the roll because a second column is where a person goes to be got at.** Batch 0015 inherits it as a thing a man said in a room and not as a thing anybody can act on.
- **Money, calendar, device counts, and the batch's length. Item 340.** 480, 704, 592, 1,104 and 102 pence footed, all in pence on the page. No banned sums, no Ash figures, no 456, no main-world or real-world month name and no main-world weekday in the ten chapters, and no rolling converted. Four `---` rules and zero bold standalone lines. One Gatehouse-voice line, in Chapter 139, unattributed. **The block is 35,318 words, per chapter 4316 / 3952 / 3156 / 2975 / 3959 / 2943 / 2978 / 2906 / 3724 / 4409, and was 35,560 with Chapter 138 at 3,148 until the Batch 0015 review repair pass removed two duplicated paragraphs from that chapter and took 242 words out of it. The block's money, its devices and its day map are untouched and its `the whole of` is still 3.** It is more than double Batch 0013 and four to five thousand above Batches 0011 and 0012, with no chapter under 2,906; Batch 0015 is to write full scenes and is not to treat either Batch 0013 or this block as a ceiling or a floor.

## Threads Batch 0015 carries into the Volume 03 close

Written by the Volume 03 Batch 0015 phase, cross-referenced to continuity items 345–372 and the Relationship state and the state-of-the-world ledger at the end of Chapter 150, **and amended by the Batch 0015 review repair pass, which is items 373–380 and which corrects four of the figures and two of the claims below.** **No thread below is closed.** All Batch 0014 threads stand except where this block moved them. **Volume 03 is complete at one hundred and fifty chapters and this is the last block's list; the volume close inherits it whole.**

- **The breach. Thread 82, items 238/263/283/303.** Open, unrepaired, and **worse on the page and not better.** He gives her the numbers before she asks and gives her the leg, and the one thing he cannot give her is the order of an ordinary morning in a yard, and he does not tell her that he cannot. **The third rule's final clause is now about him in a room and neither of them has said so, and that is a fifth thing in a coat and it is a habit and not a choice.** The ninth not-asking stands, entered on the sixteenth of Longlight, and **no tenth is entered, and in Chapter 150 she puts her pen down and does not write it and neither of them says why.** No apology in any form in any register. **He declines to give her a figure for the limp because a figure would be hers to keep and it is not a fact about a person.** Volume 04's chosen intimacy is bought with this and nothing buys any of it early.
- **The brothers. Thread 83, items 236/247/248/282/322. Item 322 is discharged on the page and this is the fact about it.** **The hand-over in Chapter 145 is to the crew's own authority and the guild's, with the brother named in a document and not in the room, and the three reasons are on the page: the road is not made to carry a person and cannot be, a main-world room cannot be inside a path chapter, and a yard's book holds things set.** Tovan Kest held one end of a line for a hundred and seventeen days without knowing it, in a yard, in his own body, and was asleep when his brother came up it, and his book's fifth line carries an hour and no figure and no name and the boy of fifteen was told it is nothing. **His name and his brother's name are on one fair copy on a board in a county that is not on any map, and Ferrow objected before the copy was made and would not have either name taken out, and the minute-man asked and was answered *No* and entered it exactly as given.** The spent sentence is not re-said, improved on, or alluded to as retractable. **The yard is the only relationship still getting better and it got better by costing six hundred and forty-eight pence and ninety-six bushels of salt and four unpaid men.**
- **The council and the guild. Items 307–308, 324, 325, 331, 345, 351, 359.** The mechanism is spent (item 323) and the four hundred and forty yards, the eleven yards, the trough, the pond and the hand's width are its fixed figures and none of them is restated in this block. The flood is spent (item 325). **The council has now broken its own oath in public, by the men who swore it, on a day it named a rolling ahead, and it has given away a road in a form of seven columns, and the guild holds the mile of boundary and a footbridge with a bay hanging off it, and its debts are 4,632 pence of which the county has none.** The re-cutting cost 1,752 and the difference from the fifth rolling's 480 is 1,272, and that difference is the cost of doing a thing twice.
- **The refused working. Item 310.** Named once and refused once in Chapter 124 and not re-derived in Chapters 131–140. **The string `cut` occurs twelve times in Chapters 141–150 as `cut` or `cutting` and three more times inside `cutwater`, and none of the fifteen is the working** (item 372). Stage 3 is not reached, attempted, offered, priced or described as available anywhere in the block, and the cut is not named.
- **The sixty and the not asking, and the four words nobody will write. Items 311–312, 318, 338, 346, 348, 350.** **Twenty-nine of fifty-eight households put a mark on a list of households to be helped and one took a mark off, and the mark is the household's own thumb, and the man of sixty-eight's is a thumb and not the flood-year smudge on the roll.** The list is keyed to the roll and **the council entered the danger of that at the head of its own list in the minute-man's hand and did it anyway, because a household that is helped by a list nobody can join to a roll is a household that is not helped at all.** The four gateway answers paid and not by deception (item 347). **The four true words of the man of sixty-eight remain in nobody's book by Aren's own decision and were not said again.** Nobody asked the sixty what they swore and nobody in this block did.
- **The gateway, the boy of eleven, and four answers in no book. Items 319, 339, 347.** **The plant paid: half of four villages signed, and not one of them was deceived, and the county has not learned it was wrong about a list and does not learn it in this block.** The four answers are uncontradicted, unimproved and in no book. Ferrow has said in Chapter 142 that a county which has been right in a gateway and is then shown a board with a thumbhole in it is a county whose people do not need to be frightened into a thing they have already agreed with. **And the line that closes the beat: a thumb on a board is a household saying it would like seed, and a household that would like seed is a household that will still be on white ground in four years if the water does not come down, and he is certain of that and has no idea what to do with it.**
- **The vault, and three marks on the outside of it. Items 313, 320, 329, 349.** Nine jars back on a shelf in the order they have always stood in, a wall with two answers built up, **three bits of tin that nobody has read, in a measure the keeper keeps in her head.** **All three parts of her want are now on the page, once, in her own words: nine jars and not a wall, the last keeper of a seed room and not the first of a seed house, and no thanks.** The one sentence §14 assigned to her is said once in Chapter 142 and is not improved on: *a list of households to be helped you can take back, and a list of persons to be struck you cannot, because there was never anybody at the other end of it to take it back to.* **Her name is spent for the fourth time and Volume 03's name budget is closed at seven of seven with twenty-three of twenty-three unfilled.** **Her decision about the three marks is a different thing and is not part of it: she has decided nothing, she has said she will decide in the spring or she will not, the spring is now, the volume is over, and the close may not decide it for her in either direction.**
- **The league and the proposal. Items 314–315, 345.** **Gace's oath that can be broken has been built into a form, tested nine days after it was made, and kept, and Ferrow's word *dangerous* was taken back in a room and not in a gateway.** The consequence is a list of households to be helped and the list landed. **The finding in item 337 landed on the same ground and the Marches is the sixth of the eight, and the promise and the release are the only door this county has, and the door is now open and the county is a place a record gathers in because it has a name.** Gace is alive, holds no office and is not on the oath, and the league still wants a man to be able to say what he will not plant without losing a field for it.
- **The flooded county. Items 325, 327, 328, 339, 351, 361.** **The plan held and one thing did not, and the thing that did not hold was a five-yard two-bay elm footbridge cut into a bank in the flood in the seventh rolling, re-cut in the ninth, and re-cut once more in the wrong place by a crew on four days' notice, which took the whole night river for nine hours because it was a lid.** Nobody died and the number of men on the bank is thirty and the reason is a ditch, a bank, nine days of work and a man on a lid. **The salt is not lifted, the first crop off two of the four white villages is a failure, and it is on a board in a keeper's hand with the hour on it, and the water table comes down over four years and not one year sooner.** The drawknife man is nine days gone and nobody in that county whose job it was hauled up four feet of bank.
- **Ash's fourth term, the correction, and the notice. Items 316, 333, 334, 335, 364.** The fourth term was offered wrongly and declined; the two-line fair copy's source is known to him and not to the county. **The County of Ash is closed in writing by a four-line notice, itemised, checkable, in a fair hand, sent by the woman who keeps that end for a bench that asked, and its fourth line is the second of the county's own terms given back whole, forty-one words, a refusal of him, and correct.** He called the route once and the answer was the notice. He holds two routes: Bellweather-Living and the Hearth Marches. **The second of the terms is referred to in Chapter 148's letter and is not re-worded and is not reprinted. `scheduled day` remains barred. Dain's offer is refused in writing in Chapter 148 and the refusal is a document and not a scene and not an apology.**
- **The withdrawal and the bar. Items 321, 330, 331, 357.** He entered that he would bring nothing else to that chamber unless asked by name; Ferrow named it aloud in front of eleven people; the breaking is in the council's book with the hour against no person; the command was the old move and it was right and it was refused in nine seconds. **The withdrawal is now discharged, and the discharge is on the page: the minute-man read the eighth rolling out of the book before the thirteenth could be filled in and entered that the man bringing this one was not asked for it, because a man who is not asked is not to ask is a rule about carrying things up a stair and giving is the other direction.** A fair copy on a board says a stranger holds no office and may not be given one, and it is still there, and the hand-over was done in a room that had already refused him one and did not pretend the first had not happened.
- **The finding, and the roll. Items 336, 337, 338.** Spent in Chapter 140 and not restated in this block. **It is in one book in one county, against nobody, with the hour, and it is in nobody's book four hundred miles off, and the keeper refused to put it in the roll because a second column is where a person goes to be got at, and the class-of-persons entry she got at her own asking in the ninth rolling is in the thirteenth the reason nobody asked a stranger about his family.** The finding is now doing work in the volume close: the Marches is a place a record gathers in because it has a stone, and a stone is a name.
- **The delegation, the account, and the company. New in this block. Items 365, 366, 367, 368.** **A fair copy of four hundred and eight words and a working came down the line addressed to a shelf and is the first thing that has ever come into that office out of a place that had agreed to be checked and had not been asked to.** Nine hundred and forty-one at the head is wrong and is struck with the reason. **A seed-and-bridge company named the nine jars and the ditch is entered by a body corporate that can be sued, with a date and a name and a witness at each end, and the two witnesses have never met and cannot be produced to each other.** Its first act is a four-line letter for a jar, sent on the twenty-first, **not answered inside the year and not going to be answered, because the road to that county is closed in four lines of writing and the person who used to hold the other end of anything there was struck and the county did not replace them in time.** **The delegation cannot arrive, because the first of the Marches' six terms is that the road is not to be made to carry a person, and the Archive has resolved to hear it on the Marches' own terms and entered the hearing for the ninth of Rainmonth of the year following, and the resolution says the delegation is not to be sent for and that if it does not come the hearing is not adjourned and not refused and it stands.** Nobody in that room will be able to tell anybody in four years whether anybody came.
- **The Marches' account and a wrong figure, and a coincidence refused. New in this block. Item 365.** **The Marches' own figures are three and none of them is a hearth, and the figure at the head of their account is a count of hearthstones that is wrong, and the same number is the only figure this office holds about the county of clay, and the two are the same by accident.** She wrote down that it is a coincidence and asked every person in the room to hear the word and not improve on it and did one single thing with it. **A later phase may not build anything on the two nines, and may not print them in one line, and 9-4-1 may not appear in a Marches scene or in a sum with a Marches figure.**
- **Dain's offer, refused in writing. New in this block. Item 364.** Four hundred and eleven words, dated, with the hour, in a bag, cut open along the left edge. **The rank is refused as a command and not a custody, the sealed record is refused as a record a person cannot see, the missing number of persons on the sheet is the load-bearing paragraph, the warrant is not to be fetched, the eastern paths are not to be closed by him, the earlier reply in a bag is not withdrawn, and there is no thanks in it.** **The refusal is not an apology and is not a scene and the marginal instruction is not explained in it and the word custodian does not appear in it. The offer is now dead in a form and the form is in a mail sack behind a salt loft, and Mara Vey does not know it exists.**
- **The figures, the devices, and the length. Item 372.** **34,113 words**, per chapter 3408 / 3368 / 2794 / 3183 / 2912 / 3014 / 3380 / 3634 / 5125 / 3295, **all four corrected at the Batch 0015 review repair pass, which found the block total had been recorded as 34,117 and Chapter 147 as 3,377 against the page's 3,380 — the number a later grep gets is 34,113.** Zero `---` rules, one bolded line and it is inside a document. No System panel and no Gatehouse-voice line. **Money foots three times on the page and once more as a working: 1,752, the difference of 1,272, 864 in salt, and 4,632.** No 4720, no 456, no 254, no 2,450, no 2,906, no hearth figure in a Marches scene. No main-world month, real-world month or main-world weekday in Chapters 141–147. **`the whole of` is 2 and `about N seconds` is 6**, both corrected at the same pass, the two survivors of the first string being Chapter 146 line 83 and Chapter 147 line 35. Filler counts as whole words: `nodded` 0, `held` 18, `wrote` 38, `stood` 12, `plain` 1, `counted` 10, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0.
- **Two defects found in earlier chapters, one of them now repaired and one of them still the volume close's. This bullet is superseded by continuity items 373–380 and is kept so that the record shows what was found and when.** **First, `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0138.md` had a paragraph repeated word for word — the cost of the eighth rolling, then a second copy of it — and a second repetition the finding did not name, in which the minute-man's speech about keeping a thing in the same hand and the same book and the same run of the page was spoken twice in a row. Both were removed at the Batch 0015 review repair pass; the reading that stands is the one saying *two flights of that stair*, the cost paragraph stands once at line 55, and the chapter is seventy-five lines.** **Second, and still open: `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0138.md` dates the guild's plan to the twelfth rolling and says the eleventh is the rolling before the man with the bar came up the stair, at its lines 11, 13 and 43, and `chapter-0149.md` line 31 calls it *the plan of the twelfth rolling* in the working, which cannot be squared with Chapter 139 in the ninth rolling with four rollings left in the year. Batch 0015 read the block as the tenth to the thirteenth per `outline/volume-03.md` §17, restated no arrival rolling, and touched no outline and no earlier chapter, and the repair pass left it alone because a fix has to move the plan's date earlier in four places in two chapters and choose a rolling before the Water, which is a plan-level decision and belongs to the close. The Volume 03 close must decide it and record which it chose.** Both are recorded so that the close can decide the second and so that neither is inherited as an error with a footnote saying it is canonical.
