## Threads Batch 0003 carries into Batch 0004

Written by the Volume 04 Batch 0003 phase, cross-referenced to continuity items **442–458**, to the Relationship state at the end of Chapter 180, to the Batch 0003 entry in `state/batch-summaries.md` and to the Block 3 note in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. **No thread below is closed. No thread carried into this batch from the Volume 03 close is closed, and none of the nineteen the close handed over and none of the nineteen Batch 0001 and none of the nineteen Batch 0002 carried forward has been answered.** All of threads 82 onward stand. **The next phase is Volume 04 Batch 0004, Chapters 181–190, Block 4, "The Thread", and that phase creates its own prompt. This batch created no prompt and wrote no marker.**

**The finding this block turns on.** **A form is a machine for making an office out of a word, and the machine has been running in a valley for two hundred years with nobody in it, and the valley's only roll turned out to be that form with four columns taken out of the middle, and the answer a community gave to a request for a figure was to enter the whole count one line at a time in its own book over a generation, and nobody recognised it as a form because there is not one form in the valley for it to be recognised against. And the second asking was made on foot at nine hundred doors with the same six things in the same words and nothing written down at any of them, and it was consented to with nine against it, and the nine is three households converted into persons by a man with a rope-belt in nine seconds, and it is the first arithmetic anybody in that valley has done on its own people and it is wrong, and it is in a book in a room that cannot be served, in a hand that varies.**

1. **The form, and it is opened, and it answers nothing, and it is not to be closed.** Items 442, 443 and 458. A sheet of laid paper about a foot and a half long, a stub torn out of a pad, on the board floor of a room about four feet by six under the eaves of a byre, under a squared piece of the byre coping that a man of fifty-eight squared in the dark. **The Office of Quarters, at Grayford.** A Return of the quarter, to be sent round once in every seven years, and returned to the same, and not to be sent on. Eight printed columns, two hands, three answers, two empty columns of which one has a rule drawn across its head that has not been ruled across in about two hundred years, and a fifth column answered *none appointed* in a slow careful hand, and a third column answered *four in this year* in a reckoning this valley cannot use, and a sixth column holding a number of years that nobody in the valley converts into anything. **It is about two hundred years old and the age is on the page as an experienced reader's finding and not as a figure derived in the valley, and it is carried as two hundred years in the state files and in no chapter.** **The form opens three things and answers none of them by Chapter 180: who put it there and when, who took four columns out of the middle of the book, and whether the two hands have anything to do with the hand in the third column of the ninth spread — and Sarr says out loud in Chapter 172 that there is one line in that book he is not going to put beside that paper and that this is the whole of what is left of his six years and it does not go on a form, and no batch may schedule an explanation, a flashback, an origin, a maker or a deathbed for any of it.**

2. **The winter book is a form, and the finding is on the page, and the missing columns are not accounted for.** Item 444. Three columns of the ninth spread ruled to the same widths as three of the eight numbers on the form, the same margin, the same depth of a ruled space, about four columns taken out of the middle and a column of names put in on the older spreads, and the person who made it that way did it on purpose and did not write down that he had done it, and the reason is the four lines at the head of the first spread. **The chapter ends on the question nobody has answered: then who took four columns out of it. A later phase may not answer it without a plan-level decision this volume has not earned, and Batch 0004 does not answer it.**

3. **The four lines at the head of the first spread, read out once, and the finding they complete.** Item 445. A rule about keepers, a hundred and forty years old, the answer to the form's fifth column, written by a man who had read that column on the floor of the back room, and it says that whoever keeps the book answers for the book and the room and the day and the mark and the hand, and for no person in the valley, and has been appointed by no person in the valley, and that wherever a man is named in a book of this valley it is because he did it himself. **The volume's finding is completed in this block: this valley spent nine generations confusing four words for a person, the confusion is not a local error but an inherited one, and the form behind the byre is where it was written down.** **A carrier is a person who has gone out of the valley and been named for it; the hand is a person who keeps a thing not made of words; a keeper maintains a place or its records, which is employment and not authority; and a fourth word, for a person a community appoints to answer for one other named person, who answers to the people who appointed him and can be relieved by them. THE WORD `CUSTODIAN` DOES NOT APPEAR IN CHAPTERS 171 TO 180 and the prompt's allowance to name it was not taken, and the fourth word is described in its own words in Chapter 173 on the Chapter 156 and Chapter 166 precedent. No character in this batch calls a carrier a custodian and no character calls a custodian a carrier. Batch 0004 may not re-derive this finding and may not improve on the four lines.**

4. **Osgar Vell, 22, and the volume's name budget.** Item 446. Named once, in a room, in Chapter 173, because he came up nine steps to say his own name and had not been asked a question since a market day. **He is the young man of the doorway, the market and the back row in Chapters 166 and 170, and the page now says so, and no character in Batch 0002 said so and this batch does not apologise for it.** Volume 04 stands at **four names of six and twenty-two of twenty-four slots unfilled**, and Batch 0004's row is one name and five slots unfilled and Batch 0005's row must be corrected to one name and four unfilled by the phase that writes it, because 4 + 5 + 4 is thirteen and twenty-two + five + four is thirty-one and the cap is thirty, and a later phase must record the overrun rather than discover it. **The three households that said no, their nine people, and the three reasons, are now on the page, and the three families are the first three to leave in Block 5, and neither the three nor the nine may be changed by a later phase.**

5. **The eighth spelling, and the two accounts, and the stroke in the wall.** Items 447 and 458. Seven words in nine miles and one description on a form, and the description is the only one of the nine that is true about anybody and nobody has ever used it. A stranger with nine beasts who gave a word in the low country's language and went on down the valley the same afternoon, and a family's correction that it was not a stranger but somebody in the valley who had it off one, and a brother of nineteen with a cold chisel off a roof slate in nine minutes in a wet week, who is seventy-four, who has never said why, and who said it once, to a stranger, about fifty-five years late, and who says that there were three of them that week and that the two people who have cut a stroke in a wall in that valley in a hundred and forty years had between them not one idea. **The two accounts of where a word came from have never been in the same room and no instrument in this kingdom can put them in one, and a man of thirty-two is the only person he has met who could check either and cannot check a word out of a man's mouth. A knife and a cold chisel in a yard is said once and is not improved on.**

6. **The winter is held by something and the something is straining, and the counterweight, and the mechanism is spent.** Items 448 and 454. **The mechanism was said once in Chapter 164, in trade terms, by a man with no stake in the Marches, and is not restated anywhere in Chapters 171 to 180, and Wystan Roole is shown knowing it by beginning a sentence and stopping.** The counterweight is a different fact and is said once: **a thing is not known to hold until it is put under a load, and everything in this valley has been under a load for nine generations and not one of it has ever been tested.** The strain as the page takes it: a bridgekeeper who can put his hand on a nine-week-old wall and know it and cannot on a stone he laid four days ago, and four hundred yards of the Burn gone quiet below a ford and not above it, and a man with a cart coming over the pass in the warm week and nothing expected of him. **Nobody in this block names the working and nobody explains it, and Block 4 may not explain it either.** Block 4's own beat is the second stand at the ford and the nine weeks of craft, and it is not in this batch.

7. **The second asking, the consent, the nine, and the word that is the word for a bridge.** Items 449, 450 and 451. Done. **The six things are on the page in full, said at about nine hundred doors in the same words, and nothing was written down at any of them, and the fifth of the six — the count in a file two hundred miles off being the only thing in the world that can be produced against anybody in this valley, and taking the winter out of the ground not touching it — is the reason the consent is not a cure and was never going to be one.** Three figures went into the book on the twenty-fourth day of the second cold in the day column of the last leaf of the ninth spread, in a hand that varies, and no name went beside the nine. **The word is the word the valley has for a bridge, it is on the page in ordinary trade use in Chapter 176, and it is not translated, and the volume says so in a document in Chapter 180 in a torn leaf out of the man's own notebook that is now on the seat of the back bench of the room over the cross.** **A later phase may not re-ask, may not re-ask in any form, and may not put the consent and a cut together.** The eleven people over a pass who cannot be asked, and about four of them will come back and seven will not, and the seven are the only people in the world who could say no, is **open and is the block's last cost and is not resolved in this block.**

8. **The return stitch, and Block 4's device, and nothing in this batch foreshadows it.** Item 454. **The return stitch is not mentioned once in Chapters 171 to 180.** It was prepared in Batch 0001 in the worst possible place on purpose, in daylight, in front of about twenty men, on the outside face of a public parapet at the height of a thumb on the weather side, and a girl of about eleven put her hand flat on it, and it is not secure. **Batch 0004 is where that is said and where a bridge parapet in this valley is taken down properly by a man of thirty-four who explains why and is not wrong, and nothing in this batch may be read as scheduling it, and the volume's second cut-to-the-north device was not spent again here.** The strand is nine weeks and it is the first time in a hundred and eighty chapters a man is stuck somewhere with no way home, and the volume does not resolve it with a second road.

9. **The drift, and the errand of the error, and the notebook.** Item 455 and Batch 0002's item 428. He went out at the fourth hour of the eighth of Goatmonth, YR 313, a Friday, not a market day, on a line made up in nine minutes with the hour against each of the nine. **The printed warning is three weeks, unchanged, in an office's hand for two years, and no character in this block uses the word *failed* about a road and no character describes the warning as having failed.** **He is still not writing a drift figure and this block does not touch the two unreconciled numbers, three weeks and two sleeps at the fourth week and the valley's weeks, and neither is converted.** He tore a leaf out of the back of the notebook in Chapter 180 and gave it away, and the rest of the book is still in a coat, and the notebook is the only record of a crossing and a concealment in one book. **Main world: absence 192 days, the twentieth of Harvestmonth, YR 314, a Monday, not a market day; error 171 days against the printed three weeks, about nine tenths of the absence; the ratio is error over absence and never over the warning and the path interval is nowhere in it. No character in this volume is told any of that figure.**

10. **The Office of Quarters at Grayford, and the office in the file, and the two that are not joined.** Items 442 and 453. The ancestor is named, and it is a different office two hundred years older, and **no character joins the two in one sentence and the join is left to the reader.** The nine words at the foot of the modern printed paper are the same nine words as at the foot of the form under the byre, and he says the wording and says he has read it before and does not describe the other one, and the reason is that a man who describes a document to nine people has made nine copies of a thing nobody in that valley can see. **The marginal instruction is not explained and is not in anybody's mouth. The Office of Provincial Registration is an office, it is dangerous because it is doing its job, and it does not appear. `outline/series.md`'s Volume 4 escalation line and knowledge-control row remain unsatisfied, are carried forward as an open item, and no later phase may read Batch 0003 as having satisfied them.** Thread 89 and the Volume 02 close's §7.1 still hold the second severance for Chapter 894, and the separate-sheet rule still stands.

11. **Dain's offer, dead in a form, and the number of persons now asked for in a return.** Batch 0002's item 401, unchanged and not in this block. **The four hundred and eleven words came back cut along the left edge with a knife and are in the Archive's press entire, uncopied, with the cut band beside them, and the clause that landed is the number of persons the offer was made out to, which is not stated on the sheet, and the number has not been supplied and is not supplied in this block.** Her want is unchanged and she is not the person who explains the marginal instruction and she is not in this block in a room. **The second copyist's want, a post with her name in the day-book, is still unmet and is not met in this block.** **Batch 0004's main-world block is the one that gets a bench of nine asked to fund a road with a figure footed in pence and a hearing that stands empty, and neither of those things is staged or priced here.**

12. **The hearing, and what a body corporate owes for a hearing against nobody.** Batch 0002's item 429, unchanged and referred to nowhere in this block. Entered for the ninth of Rainmonth, YR 314, a Tuesday, at the second hour, not a market day, against no person, for a delegation that is not to be sent for. It was held on its day, nobody came, nobody was sent, it was not adjourned and not refused, and the keeper wrote four lines and did not write a fifth. **The decision-document is `outline/volume-04.md` §8 Block 4's page and no batch may write it before Block 4.** **There is no main-world room in Chapters 171 to 180 and the hearing is not staged, held, adjourned, refused or referred to in this block, and he was not in that county and has sent nothing and nothing has come from him, and she has not asked, and it is not a question for a minute, and the ninth not asking stands and this block does not enter a tenth.**

13. **The breach, the ninth not asking, and the third rule's last clause.** Batch 0002's item 415 and the Relationship state at the end of Chapter 180. **There is no main-world room in this block, no cut to the north, and no reference to her by any character, and the two of them are not in one sentence anywhere in these ten chapters and no instrument in this case can put them in one.** The third rule's final clause is still about him and neither of them has ever said so and this block did not make either of them say so. No apology in any form and no mistake has been made available to anybody. **The Volume 04 chosen intimacy is not bought and is Block 4's and Block 5's, and the cost of it is a thing that has to be paid and not a cure.** **The second not-asking, a man at a gate who will not say what a word means, was not turned into a mirror of the first and no character in this block noticed that the two resemble anything, and the four words for a person in Chapter 173 are about a valley and not about him.**

14. **The brothers and the yard.** Thread 83, item 322 discharged, untouched. **The yard is off the page for all ten chapters of this block and that absence is a cost and not a convenience.** One exchange in a gateway, six words, in Batch 0001, and the word brother not used. **No Volume 04 scene may heal the estrangement, re-teach him an ordinary morning, or put the word brother into a room the two of them are in, and the spent sentence is not re-said, improved on or alluded to as retractable.** A man of seventy-four and his brother who went out of the valley and came home and is dead are a family in a path and are not this.

15. **The tile on the coping, the bell, and the far end.** Not mentioned once in this block. **The tile is not explained, its device is named by nobody, and no batch may schedule an explanation, a flashback, an origin, a maker or a deathbed.** The bell has forty-one rivets and a dull red seam and goes at the fourth hour and a half, rung by a boy and a carpenter, and a Monday is the day that far end keeps and the crossing out was a Friday, and the two facts are in a day-book separately and are not in one sentence. Nothing in the sound of it is his. **The road to the Marches' end of the line stayed shut and was not reopened and nothing in this block reopened it.**

16. **The seed letter, the four-line notice, and the two bare inches.** **The letter for a jar went to a post office and has not been delivered and is not answered in this block, and no living holder of the other end was found and none may be.** The four-line notice is not an answer to anything because nothing was asked. **The two inches of bare paper at the right-hand edge of a list of things sent and the blank ruled column in a bench's minute are not filled and not ruled in, and a later writer may not fill either.** The list of things sent has no fifth column and the second copyist has still not been given a post with her name in the day-book.

17. **The three marks on the outside of the seed vault, and the keeper's decision.** Not mentioned once in this block and the decision is not taken. **The spring is a date in the Marches' own calendar and not in this one's, and Volume 04 and every volume after it may not take that decision from her.**

18. **The standing prohibitions, all verified in this block and all standing.** No 456, no 254, no 2,450, no 2,906, no 9-4-1 in any form, and the two nines hundred and forty-ones not printed in one line; the four thousand and the seven hundred and twenty never added; the Marches' six terms not re-worded; the county's form seven columns; the five blanks of Volume 02 not joined; the eighth ruled line not looked at; the brine weights neither mentioned nor filed; *Culling Order* does not appear; *Gatehouse* does not appear; **the word *custodian* does not appear in Chapters 171 to 180 and the prompt's allowance to name the fourth word was not taken**; Sian Pugh's name not spoken; Edric Hunn not written anywhere and not in a column and not in a room; the two offices never joined in one sentence; no Stage 3, no lineage, no named working, no named cut; no tenth not-asking; no apology; no main-world month, no real-world month and no main-world weekday in any valley scene in any register including a notebook.

19. **The figures, the devices and the length, and what the next writer must not re-derive.** Item 455. **34,037 words, per chapter 3306 / 3277 / 3351 / 3711 / 3938 / 3349 / 3230 / 3139 / 3303 / 3433; Volume 04 is 100,467 in thirty chapters; the manuscript is 702,109 in one hundred and eighty chapters.** `---` rules **16**, one per chapter and a second in six of them; bolded line-openings **49**; semicolons **0**; em-dashes **0**; straight double quotes **0**; curly apostrophes **0**; **`the whole of` 3**, against Batch 0002's 36, **and the first pass of this block carried it at 35 and the writer went back through all ten chapters twice and cut it out of twenty-eight places, and a later writer should carry 3 as the number and should not go looking for the phrase**; `about N seconds` **0**; `cut` **21** and all twenty-one ordinary and the working not among them and not named; no System panel and no Gatehouse-voice line. Filler as whole words, a baseline and not a target: **`nodded` 0, `held` 8, `wrote` 28, `stood` 36, `plain` 0, `counted` 10, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0.** **`about` is 394 and runs 42 / 33 / 35 / 43 / 37 / 39 / 37 / 43 / 41 / 44, and no chapter is over forty-five and the density does not compound.** **Money foots once on the page, in pence, before the total is said: 3, sixteen times over, is 48, 48 eleven times over is 528, 4 for a barrow of salt, and 528 and 4 is 532 pence, which is eleven marks and fourpence; and the valley's own price of that fortnight is eleven kitchens and about four barrows of salt and nobody's figure, and the two are never added, and the form's printed figure of *four in this year* is not converted into anything.** **Three calendar defects in this block's own text, all the same defect, all found and repaired: a weekday spoken aloud in a valley room, a main-world month name spoken by a character about the day he came out on, and a main-world month name spoken about the last Crown's hand in a county. That is the seventh, eighth and ninth time in this project that carrying a figure instead of counting it has produced a main-world calendar in a valley scene, and the rule is unchanged: a later phase states a figure, checks it, and records the check, and a state file is the least reliable document in this repository.** **And no chapter of this block ends on a refusal, and no chapter ends on a sentence that is very nearly an aphorism, and four men decline to do things in the ten chapters and four chapters are built on a refusal and not one of them is a last line — that is the block's deliberate answer to `outline/volumes/volume-03-close.md` §10 items 1 and 2, and Batch 0004 is the block where a reader is most likely to expect the rhythm back and should be told that the middle of this volume has already spent it.**

**The threads this batch opened, and it closes none of them.** **One:** the four columns that were taken out of the middle of the form, and who took them out, and the fact that nobody has ever written down that they were taken out. **Two:** the two hands — the slow careful one that wrote three answers and stopped, and the office's hand that does not vary across two answers written on different days — and Sarr's own hand in the ninth spread, and the fact that the three of them have never been in a room together and that he has said he is not going to put them there. **Three:** the two accounts of where a word came from, and no instrument in this kingdom that can check either. **Four:** the nine, which is three households converted into persons in nine seconds and is wrong, and is the first arithmetic anybody in that valley has done on its own people. **Five:** the page torn out of a notebook and put on a bench, which is the first object that has ever been on one of the twenty-nine benches and the first thing anybody in that valley has ever written down that says what a thing means.

---

## Threads Batch 0004 carries into Batch 0005

Written by the Volume 04 Batch 0004 phase, cross-referenced to continuity items **459–476**, to the Relationship state at the end of Chapter 190, to the Batch 0004 entry in `state/batch-summaries.md` and to the Block 4 note in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. **No thread below is closed. No thread carried into this batch from the Volume 03 close is closed, and none of the nineteen the close handed over and none of the nineteen Batch 0001 and none of the nineteen Batch 0002 and none of the nineteen Batch 0003 carried forward has been answered.** All of threads 82 onward stand. **The next phase is Volume 04 Batch 0005, Chapters 191–200, Block 5, "The Seam", and that phase creates its own prompt. This batch created no prompt and wrote no marker.**

**The finding this block turns on.** **A place can take a thing away from a man who gave it to them, properly, in daylight, with the reason said out loud twice before a bar goes on the stone, and be right, and the man can agree with it in about four seconds and be sorry about none of it, and the cost of the agreement is that he is stuck in a valley that can now be left and that he cannot, for nine weeks, with no second mark and no second road and nobody in four hundred miles building him a way out — and the nine weeks are a training block in which a master repairer in four counties fails at a valley's craft in public twice, is corrected by a man nine years his junior, is told his own count is not this valley's count, is not allowed to lay a stone in the valley at all, and ends up counting load points for somebody else for the first time in his life.** And four hundred miles off a bench of nine says no to a road with forty marks on a page over a chest with two marks in it, and a body corporate that can be sued writes down what it owes for a hearing against nobody, and the seventh column of the finding is empty, and the empty column is the finding.

1. **The return stitch, and this block is where it went.** Item 459. **The mark was in the coping of the far abutment of the stand at the Low Holm, a line and a bar and a figure, at the height of a thumb, on the weather side, cut in the third cold in front of sixteen men by a man who cut it for the reason that a place you come back to has to be a place you can stand in.** The valley had been looking at it for nine weeks and had not acted. **Roole laid that coping himself in nine minutes and did not know the mark was there until the second day of the fourth cold, and he said so in a room before he gave any other reason, and he asked the man who cut it one question in front of nine people and got one word.** The taking down was to a whole course of nine stones and not to the mark, in daylight, with the nine sentences said first in a room of eleven and again in a cutting to about forty people, and nobody rubbed it out. **The nine stones are in the footing of the second stand at the ford, under about four feet of water, in the order they came off, and the mark is still readable in flat light for about four months.** The new coping is nine inches and is the valley's own stone and in nine years nobody will be able to tell the two apart. **This thread is answered and closed inside itself and no later phase may reopen it, and the place is a bridge parapet in a valley and a valley took it down.**

2. **The stranding, and it is nine weeks, and it is not resolved.** Items 459, 462. **He is stuck. He is not getting out before the warm week, the man of thirty-four said so out loud once in nine sentences with no figure on it, and the reason he had said nothing for nine weeks is that a man who has just been told he is stuck is not a man you can give a job to.** **The man of thirty-four thought about building a road on the first of the four days and did not, and the reason is on the page: a road to the top of this bank that a man did not ask for is not a road out of this valley, it is a road to a place where a man stands, and nothing at the other end of it has been agreed to by anybody in this valley, and he has spent nine days taking a stranger's mark out of a public stone for exactly that reason.** **No second mark, no second line, no second witness, no preparation, no offer, and nobody in the valley or in the main world built him a way out in this block, and the volume does not resolve it with a second road, and a road that does not open and a road that fails are not confused anywhere in Chapters 181 to 190.**

3. **The mark in the Sedge Reach coping, four hundred miles off, and it is not the way out.** Item 459. **It is the same mark, cut with the same chisel at the ninth hour of the sixth of Goatmonth, YR 313, in front of about twenty men, and a girl of about eleven put her hand flat on it.** It is on the page in Chapter 182 and in the man's own interior, it is not in the taking down, it was not touched in this block, nobody in this valley knows it exists, and **it is not going to be the way out and no later batch may use it as one and nothing explains it.**

4. **The nine weeks, and the sixty-three days, and the two failures, and the load points, and the wages.** Items 463 and 464. **Sixteen men and about thirty under the cross, a mill, nine roofs the ninth generation will not let them put up, four hundred yards that is not a road, and a second stand at the ford. The first public failure is the load count on the ninth day of the third week, the second is the floor in the mill trough on the forty-first day, and both are corrected by the man of thirty-four and by a man of about fifty-one and neither is corrected by the narrator.** The wages are 3,032 pence in a county's money, sixty-three marks and eightpence, footed on the page, and the valley's own price is not a figure and the two are not added. **The block's word is `counted` and it is up at 24 against Batch 0003's 10, and a later writer should read that as the block's trade word and not as a tic.**

5. **The second stand at the ford, and where the nine stones go when the ford moves.** Items 461 and 476. **The ford moves about a foot a year and the man of thirty-four has known that for nine years and has never once said it in a room, and the nine stones of the old coping are in a footing that will not be where the ford is in nine years, and nobody in that valley has worked out where it goes.** **This thread is opened and held and is not scheduled to be answered, and if a later phase answers it the answer must be a trade fact and not a moral one and must not be a discovery.**

6. **Torrin Hatch, 55, and the promise that is nobody's to hold, and the aisle.** Items 465 and 466. **He is the last man in the valley who has ever stood in the aisle, he stood in it thirty-five years ago at twenty, he voted against keeping the winter and lost eight to one, he said no more about it, and he came to the room four more times in nine years and did not stand in the aisle at any point, and that is all that is known about him and it is all he wanted.** **He is right about the history and wrong about the soil and no character in this batch called him a mirror and no character called him a villain and no character called him anything.** **The aisle over the cross has now gone three generations without being used, the twenty-nine benches are still bolted to the floor with a number cut into the end of each one, a hundred and forty is cut into the end of the one at the back of the right-hand row, the number has not moved in about nine years, nobody has ever been struck off one of them, and a barrow went out through the four feet of the aisle on two wheels at the end of the second morning and the man who pushed it had not been asked.** **Nev Treen of the eighth generation has offered in writing in a minute that cannot be served to stand in that aisle at sixty and be asked what she swore, and the ninth generation will watch, and in nine years one of the tenth will be in it and none of the nine of the council will have to be in it twice, and no third asking of the ninth generation may be staged by any later batch.**

7. **The council's second question, and the four words.** Item 465. **What happens if it does not keep the winter. Put nine times, answered four times, and the clerk wrote *no answer* five times in a valley with no writ and no bell and no office. The answer is in the minute in four words: *No answer in the room.* And the reason nobody can give it is that the thing underneath has been held nine generations and there is no book about it that anybody has read, and the only document anybody has ever been sent is a two-hundred-year-old form about the number of men going out and it is not about a winter, and the eight columns were read out again in a cold room in Chapter 186 and used rather than rediscovered.**

8. **The subscription sentence in a valley minute, and nobody in that valley knows it is a quotation.** Item 467. **Said by Torrin Hatch about forty seconds into the second morning, written out by a clerk of about sixty who did not ask where he had it from, in a minute that cannot be served, with four people in the room noticing that nobody asked and nine not noticing, and Iven Sarr saying that in nine years a woman who is not in that room is going to read it and believe it was invented here, and she will be right.** **The sentence is word for word and is not improved on and no character may ever say where it came from, and this thread is opened and held.**

9. **The two figures of the ninth generation that do not agree with each other.** Item 476. **A woman of about thirty took a slate out from round her neck and said a figure she has kept for eleven years, and the ninth spread of the only roll has about two hundred of the ninth generation in it, and the two figures are not the same, and neither was struck out and neither was offered up to be corrected, and there is nowhere in nine miles that either of them can be put.** **Both are going to be in that valley for nine years and Block 5 may use both and may not reconcile them and may not strike either out.**

10. **The hand in the valise.** Item 476. **A man who makes nails was on the ground work for nine weeks of the sixty-three days and asked for it, and Roole put him on the barrow run and took him off it in four days, and the reason is in nine sentences and is not about nails: Roole had not thought about it, and then he watched the man look out past the top of the bank on the second day and every day since, and he is not going to have the man at the front of the queue for leaving working it for him in a cold while sixteen men who want the work do not get it.** **He went and put a nail in a stand for fifty-nine days. The nail man is one of the three households that said no, he has wanted to go out of this valley for nine years, and he is the first family out of the valley with the other two households, and this batch did not move him out and no later batch may.**

11. **The hearing, the finding, and what a body corporate owes.** Items 469, 470 and 471. **The document is written in seven columns on three sheets, the day and the hour go in as two things, the claim number is the number of the account, nothing is entered against a person, the seventh column is empty and the empty column is the finding, and the delegation is not sent for.** The meaning is on a second sheet, unattributed, and the finding is that a meaning in a minute can only be read and not argued with. The cost is on a third sheet and it is twelve pence, and a fourth line says the cost falls on a woman who is going to be thirty-eight in about nine years and who cannot be put in a column. **It goes on a shelf with the number against the office and nothing against a person, and a second chair in that room has not been moved since the ninth of Rainmonth and was not moved, and Block 5 may use either.**

12. **The two claims in four years and a half that are also against nobody, and the finding that hearing about it less is not the same as it being less.** Item 472. **A return answered with a figure of 2,640 in a woman's hand with no seal on it that nobody can check, and a strip of cut paper that weighs a shade over ten ounces and is the account of nobody, and the number of persons the offer was made out to is still not on the sheet and has been asked for in a return and has not come.** **The two are four hundred miles apart and neither of the people who put them there has ever heard of the other one, and a bundle of nine letters from that one county is on a second shelf with a piece of tarred hemp through it.**

13. **The breach, the ninth not asking, and the third rule's last clause.** Item 473 and the Relationship state at the end of Chapter 190. **The not-askings stand at nine and this block does not enter a tenth and puts no number on the page, and no apology in any form is available to anybody, and neither of them has said they have been wrong.** **This is the first block in the volume in which both of them are named and both are on the page, and they are never in one sentence in the same paragraph, and he is not in Chapters 188 to 190 at all, and the distance between the two facts is on the page and is not closed.** **The intimacy is bought in this block and it is bought with an accounting: a fourth line in a document about a person who cannot be named, and a deed of thirteen words said out loud in a room of two and not written down, and the finding that this office could hold a meaning and a county council four hundred miles off could not hold a road. It is not a cure and the third rule's final clause was not put near a document and neither of them said anything about it.** **The second not-asking was not turned into a mirror of the first and no character in this block noticed that the two resemble anything, and the subscription sentence in a valley minute was not made to resemble a concealment in a fen county, and the four words for a person in Chapter 173 were not re-derived.**

14. **The brothers and the yard.** Thread 83, item 322 discharged, untouched. **The yard is off the page for all forty chapters written so far in this volume and that absence is a cost and not a convenience.** One exchange in a gateway, six words, in Batch 0001, and the word brother not used between them. **No Volume 04 scene may heal the estrangement, re-teach him an ordinary morning, or put the word brother into a room the two of them are in, and the spent sentence is not re-said, improved on or alluded to as retractable.** The only use of the word in this block is a man of about twenty-four of the ninth generation talking about his grandfather's brother and a roof, and a man of thirty-four talking about a roof being for a family.

15. **The tile on the coping, the bell, and the far end.** Not mentioned once in this block. **The tile is not explained, its device is named by nobody, and no batch may schedule an explanation, a flashback, an origin, a maker or a deathbed.** The bell has forty-one rivets and a dull red seam and goes at the fourth hour and a half, rung by a boy and a carpenter, and a Monday is the day that far end keeps and the crossing out was a Friday, and the two facts are in a day-book separately and are not in one sentence. Nothing in the sound of it is his. **The road to the Marches' end of the line stayed shut and was not reopened and nothing in this block reopened it, and a carrier of Merefen has been up the low country twice in eleven weeks and has been to a valley and has not been to that end of anything.**

16. **The seed letter, the four-line notice, and the two bare inches.** **The letter for a jar went to a post office and has not been delivered and is not answered in this block, and no living holder of the other end was found and none may be.** The four-line notice is not an answer to anything because nothing was asked. **The two inches of bare paper at the right-hand edge of a list of things sent and the blank ruled column in a bench's minute are not filled and not ruled in, and the list has no fifth column, and the second copyist has still not been given a post with her name in the day-book and has said once in a room of two that she is not going to get it and does not want it and that the two are the same thing.**

17. **The three marks on the outside of the seed vault, and the keeper's decision.** Not mentioned once in this block and the decision is not taken. **The spring is a date in the Marches' own calendar and not in this one's, and Volume 04 and every volume after it may not take that decision from her.**

18. **The standing prohibitions, all verified in this block and all standing.** No 456, no 254, no 2,450, no 2,906 and no 9-4-1 in any form, and the two nines hundred and forty-ones not printed in one line; the four thousand and the seven hundred and twenty never added; the Marches' six terms not re-worded and not quoted; the county's form seven columns and the day and the hour two of them; the five blanks of Volume 02 not joined; the eighth ruled line not looked at; the brine weights neither mentioned nor filed; *Culling Order* does not appear; *Gatehouse* does not appear; **the word *custodian* does not appear in Chapters 181 to 190 and no character calls a carrier a custodian and no character calls a custodian a carrier**; Sian Pugh's name not spoken; Edric Hunn not written anywhere and not in a column and not in a room; the two offices never joined in one sentence; no Stage 3, no lineage read, no named working, no named cut, no cut scheduled or priced or foreshadowed; no tenth not-asking; no apology; **no main-world month, no real-world month and no main-world weekday in any valley scene in any register including a notebook, and the block's own clock is the ninth day of the fourth cold to about the ninth week of it.**

19. **The figures, the devices and the length, and what the next writer must not re-derive.** **Item 474 as first written, superseded on every figure by item 477 and by the Batch 0004 Review Repair Pass; a next writer foots from 477 and not from 474.** **34,011 words as repaired at the Batch 0004 review, per chapter 3393 / 3213 / 3105 / 3505 / 3589 / 3559 / 3433 / 3445 / 3631 / 3138; Volume 04 is 134,478 in forty chapters; the manuscript is 736,120 in one hundred and ninety chapters.** `---` rules **19**, one per chapter and a second in nine; bolded line-openings **59**; semicolons **0**; em-dashes **0**; straight double quotes **0**; curly apostrophes **0**; **`the whole of` 3**, against Batch 0003's 3, **and the first pass of this block carried it at 24 and the writer went back through all ten chapters and cut it out of twenty-one places, and a later writer should carry 3 and should not go looking for the phrase**; `about N seconds` **10**; `cut` **31** and all thirty-one ordinary and the working not among them and not named; no System panel; no Gatehouse-voice line. Filler as whole words, a baseline and not a target: **`nodded` 0, `held` 4, `wrote` 27, `stood` 34, `plain` 0, `counted` 24, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0** — **`counted` is up at 24 and it is the block's word and it is recorded rather than hidden.** **`about` is 391 and runs 39 / 44 / 40 / 43 / 45 / 42 / 41 / 38 / 36 / 23, and no chapter is over forty-five and the density does not compound, and the three main-world chapters run 38, 36 and 23.** **Two money beats, both footed in pence on the page before the total is said: the nine weeks' wages, 3,032 pence, which is sixty-three marks and eightpence, with the valley's own price not a figure and the two never added; and the carrier's road, 1,920 pence, which is forty marks, with the addition shown at every line and the answer eleven words long and given by a chest with two marks in it.** **Two defects were found in this block's own text and repaired while it was being written, a crowd counted at about four hundred in Chapter 183 where it is about forty, and three Harvestmonth dates in Chapters 188 and 189 checked against `outline/volume-04.md` §17's chain; that is the tenth time in this project that carrying a figure instead of counting it has produced a date, and the rule does not change. And a third was found and not repaired and is recorded: Batch 0003's per-chapter `about` column reads 42 / 33 / 35 / 43 / 37 / 39 / 37 / 43 / 41 / 44 for 394 and the page reads 43 / 33 / 35 / 45 / 36 / 39 / 34 / 45 / 39 / 42 for 391, and the column is annotated in continuity item 475 and is not renumbered and not edited into the Batch 0003 entry.**

**The threads this batch opened, and it closes one of them and none of the others.** **One, closed:** the return stitch, destroyed properly, in public, in daylight, with the reason given twice before a bar went on a stone, by a bridgekeeper, to a whole course and not to the mark, and the mark under four feet of water at a ford. **Two:** where the nine stones of the old coping go when the ford moves a foot a year, and the man of thirty-four has known it for nine years and has never once said it in a room. **Three:** a man of fifty-five and a sentence he cannot account for, and a clerk who did not ask, and nobody in nine years is going to know. **Four:** two figures of the ninth generation that do not agree with each other and are both going to be there in nine years. **Five:** a hand in a valise in a hall over a wharf about a barrow, which is a man in the valley who did not want to be looked at past the top of a bank and did not want to be first out of a valley he can now leave. **And the block's real cost, which is not a thread and is a fact: a man of thirty-two spent four days on a bank with a place to come back to and did not go, and the place is four hundred miles off in a county that keeps its days, and a girl of about eleven in that county put her hand flat on a mark in a parapet on a Wednesday, and neither of them knows the other one is on the same line.**

## Threads the Batch 0004 review repair pass carries into Batch 0005

**These are two new and they are craft threads and not story threads, and they exist because the review found that the volume's fastest drift was invisible to the mechanism built to catch drift. Neither is a plot thread and neither may be answered in prose; both are carried by the next writer's own counts.**

20. **`nine` is now a tracked device with a ceiling, and this is the thread the review opened and it is the most important thing it found.** Continuity item 477. **The standalone word ran 2.87 / 4.58 / 7.31 / 11.11 per 1,000 words across Batches 0001 to 0004 and is now 5.40, and the reversal only holds if the next block does not start climbing again. `about nine + noun` must not exceed four in any chapter of Batch 0005 and `in nine years` must not exceed three.** The motif is canon and may not be cut: **nine stones in a course, nine hundred doors and gates, nine generations and the ninth spread, a hundred and forty, the ninth of a month, the ninth hour, the ninth day, the bench of nine, the nine of the ninth generation, the nine that are three households, nine printed words at the foot of the two-hundred-year-old form, and a girl of nine who counts things.** The residue is what is policed, and the substitute vocabulary when a sentence wants a vague quantity is **about forty, about eleven, about a third, half, most, more than, as many again, in a few years, since the second day of this cold** — **and the deeper rule, which is that a hedge on a number the block already knows exactly is a tell that the writer was avoiding a count, and the repair made facts exact rather than deleting them.** **A batch summary that reports `nine` for this block without a per-chapter column is a summary that has not read item 477.**

21. **A chapter's bolded ledger opening may no longer deliver the chapter, and the prohibition is narrow and deliberate.** Continuity item 479. **The bolded omniscient opening paragraph is the manuscript's signature and it stays, and the block's fifty-nine of them are not being cut. What may not happen is the opening stating the answer, the verdict, the total, the vote, or the thing a character is going to say.** An opening may give the situation, the clock, the place and the pressure, and it may promise that something is coming, and it may not deliver it. **The second delivery inside a block is legitimate and is not this thread: the nine sentences are said in Chapter 181 and said again in Chapter 183, and that repetition is signposted and earns its place by adding the taking down. What was cut is the third, the opening recap, and it was cut in the three chapters where it actually spoiled something — 181, 188 and 190.** A next writer who opens with a ledger paragraph that names the verdict has written the third delivery and should cut it before the chapter is finished, not after the review finds it.

---

## Threads Batch 0005 carries into the Volume 04 close

Written by the Volume 04 Batch 0005 phase, cross-referenced to continuity items **482–497**, to the Relationship state at the end of Chapter 200, to the Batch 0005 entry in `state/batch-summaries.md` and to the Block 5 note in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. **No thread inherited is closed and none is answered. The nineteen the Volume 03 close handed over, and the nineteen each of Batches 0001, 0002, 0003 and 0004 carried forward, all stand, and all of threads 82 onward stand.** **This is the last ten chapters of Volume 04 and the volume closes at Chapter 200; the next phase is the Volume 04 close and this block created no prompt and wrote no marker.** **Three threads are closed by this block — the nine and the three households, the stranding, and the mark — and nothing else is, and every other thread below is open and is handed to the close to be held and reported and not answered.**

**The finding this block turns on.** **A community dug a hole in its own ground over four days with a stranger in it and about forty of its own hands on it, and the stranger was not allowed to lay one stone, and then the same community filled the hole in a warm week and let him judge whether a bank was square because there is no instrument in nine miles that can, and then the winter came down over eleven weeks and took four hundred yards of made ground and six byres and four roofs and a mill with it, and about two hundred people began to leave a valley that had never once wanted to be anywhere, and the first three families out were the three that said no, and nobody in that valley arranged it and nobody has been sorry about it in public. Four hundred miles off a line of about nine inches appeared in a stone course in a bridge and a man on a deck looked at it once and did not measure it, and a man came up out of that same course of stone three weeks later with a week of fever after it, and somebody else held his tools, and a document went out in the ordinary post with the word eastern in it, and a woman of twenty-nine made him say a leg and a seam and two hundred people out loud in a room of two and told him she is not going to walk up that road with him.**

1. **The cut, the four days, the face and the two words, and the question nobody asked.** Item 482. **A bar went into the bottom of a face four feet down at the ford about a finger and stopped and came back with a white line on the point of it, and a man of fifty-one said *that is the thing*, and a man of thirty-four said two words from the top of a bank and did not come down.** Nobody asked what the rope was for and nobody asked what the four days were for and about nine people in that valley were close enough to see either. **A thread opened and held and not scheduled for an answer: what a person in that valley in a few years will say the four days were, and the answer will be a story that started in the place where the hole is, and the man of thirty-four said in the open in front of forty people that there is no way in that valley to get back to the fourth day and find out.** The form of this thread is the form of the mark's and the volume has now used that argument twice, on a public stone and on a public bank, and the second use is in a different man's mouth and about a different thing and is not a restatement.

2. **The eight names in eight hands and the ninth line that is empty.** Items 482, 483 and 484. **Closed inside itself, as a fact and not as a resolution.** Eight people wrote their own names into a second column ruled on the ninth spread, because a name in a book of that valley goes in because the person did it, and the ninth line is the same depth as the other eight and is not ruled across and is not going to be. **A thread opened and held: the ninth is a person in one of the three households who was not at a door and cannot be found, and there is no column for that either, and a man of fifty-eight put a margin note in his own hand and did not rule a line.**

3. **The nine and the three households, and the leaving. CLOSED BY THIS BLOCK.** Items 483 and 495. **The three households hold eight people and the figure of nine was made in nine seconds on a step with a rope-belt on and is wrong by one, and the figure stays in the day column because it is what was said at nine hundred doors, and the margin says *eight named and one not found*.** The first three families out are the three that said no, on the fourth, the sixth and the ninth of the next cold. **No later phase may name any of the nine, may restate or improve on the three reasons given at three doors in Chapter 179, and may not move the three households out of order.** And the man of thirty-four put the man of nails off a barrow run for fifty-nine days so that he would not be at the front of the queue for leaving and has not told him, and the thread of the hand in the valise is that same man and is **open**.

4. **The stranding and the return. CLOSED BY THIS BLOCK, and the way out is not the stitch.** Items 462 and 489. **The return is the twentieth of Harvestmonth, YR 314, a Monday, not a market day, the fourth hour, made through another living anchor and not through the stitch, which was destroyed properly in Batch 0004 and is not rebuilt and is not going to be.** He comes up out of the joint between the third and the fourth course of the fourth bay of the Sedge Reach, and the witness at that end is a man of forty-seven, **who is Bram Ottery and is on the bench of nine because he was elected and not because he is on a trade and is paid fourpence a week out of a private box, which is Chapter 152's description of him and the job Chapters 156 and 157 gave him, and the reach is kept by that bench** — as repaired at item 498, and the Batch 0005 first pass called him a road warden on the roll with a vote and not an office, which is Aren Kest's own position and is not canon. **A valley that could now be left and that he could not leave for nine weeks gave him a hole to dig, a bank to be square about, four hundred yards of made ground to lose and nine hundred yards of barrow road to make, and a woman with a yoke going over a stand four times a day through his works, and a barrow run that had to go round a thing and cost eleven yards a load.** No second mark, no second line, no second witness, no preparation and no offer exist anywhere in Chapters 191 to 200, and the mark in the coping of the first bay is four yards from the course he came up in and is the same mark cut with the same chisel and he put his hand flat on it and it was cold and he said nothing to anybody, and **the volume does not join the two marks and no batch may join them and no batch may use either as a way out.**

5. **The scar, the course, the hole, the eleven yards, and the wall at the low end of a barrow run.** Item 485. **Closed as a repair and open as a fact.** The course came up in about two hours in a warm week, the hole was filled with the ground keyed to it, and the sixteen are to come back. **Held open: a mill man of sixty-one put nine barrows into that course and then said one thing to a man of fifty-one, which is that if the water comes up over that run in the warm week it will find the eleven yards on the low end first, and the eleven yards are made ground and there is not one stone in it, and then the run will be on its own with nothing holding it at the low end and it will want a wall. And a wall at the low end of a barrow run at a ford in that valley is not going to be built in the next nine years by sixteen men, and everybody at the low end of it that day knew that.**

6. **The eleven over a pass, the four, and the seven.** Items 482 and 496. **Open and not resolved and not to be resolved by a state file.** Eleven of the ninth went over the pass on the fourth day of the fourth cold; about four will come back in the first warm week; the man of thirty-four has been in that valley nine years and has never once known which four. **The seven that went with the asking in the second month of the second cold cannot be asked, have not been asked, and the consent was not made unanimous by their absence and this block did not make it unanimous.** The man of fifty-eight entered the eleven and refused to enter a figure for the other seven and said that the day a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow and a dog comes up that bank and says where her brother is, she is at a door and not at a book. **The Volume 04 close must report this thread as open and must not schedule its answer and must not reconcile it.**

7. **The winter coming down and the eleven weeks, and the figure that was not entered.** Item 487. **Open as a cost and closed as a number.** A warm week of nine days and then eleven weeks with no cold in that valley for the first time in nine generations, and a week of that valley is a thing a person standing up in a cold room says a week is, and there is no authority in that valley that could be asked, and a man of fifty-eight entered no figure of weeks in the only book in it and gave the reason in four words and nine sentences. **Held open: the four hundred yards of made ground that was the best ground anybody in that valley had ever seen is a slope, and no instrument in nine miles can tell anybody which part of it went and which part of it was always going to go.**

8. **The ninth generation's four words, and what a yes that happened on a day is.** Item 488. **The asking was not refused and the answer is *We said yes once*, entered in the middle of a page, as a fact and not as a defeat and not as a pledge.** A man of fifty-eight put it there because there is no column in that book for a thing that is a fact and not a count and he is not ruling one. **Held open, and it is the volume's live hand-off: a figure of people that somebody can check is a figure, and a figure of people that nobody can check is a rumour with a mark on it, and about two hundred people in a valley of about four hundred have begun to leave, and there is no figure for the number that are going and there is not going to be one, and a man of fifty-eight stood at the edge of a market for an hour and a half with a piece of rag paper in his hand and did not write one figure down.**

9. **The valley's memory of him, and the second sentence.** Item 495. **Open and it is not fond and it is not a villain's.** A man of about twenty-eight of the ninth generation said to a child of nine, in the open, in front of about nine people, that the man who took the winter out is the reason nine roofs are open, and that the ground he made with sixteen of them went to a slope in nine days, and that his roof is on because of the sixteen, and that the child will be told that a man came and the winter went and will not be told what it cost. **A man of fifty-eight has said out loud in a room that a sentence in that valley about a man off a road is the second one in two years and that he does not know what to do with two of anything, and the first of those two sentences is in a minute nobody can be served and the origin of it is not to be traced by anybody in this series and the Volume 04 close must not trace it.**

10. **The seam in the stone course, and the mark in the coping, and the two of them.** Items 459, 489 and 490. **Both open and neither explained and neither joined.** A line of about nine inches in the third course from the low end of the fourth bay of the Sedge Reach, in the stone and not across the joint, not got at with a nail or a cold chisel, does not move, was pointed over on the twenty-third of Harvestmonth at the joint and not at the stone, and is under about half an inch of lime. A carter of thirty found it on a Tuesday; **a man of forty-seven, who is Bram Ottery, on the bench of nine because he was elected, would not have it measured, and he is the witness at that end and not the person keeping the reach — as repaired at item 498**; the guild's man with the levels would not put his rule down and gave the sentence about a figure and a decline and a survey and a form, **in Chapter 150's wording and in his own mouth as repaired at item 499, having said it before to one man in a hall over a wharf and not to a yard in two years, and about nine men within thirty feet of it heard it and the mason of fifty and a half said, to nobody, that he had been doing it the whole time and had never once had the words** — and the first pass of this block had the sentence in the wrong mouth and re-worded it. The protagonist looked at it once and did not measure it and was offered a rule on the Thursday and did not take it. **The mark in the coping of the first bay is there since the sixth of Goatmonth, YR 313, and cannot be read at thirty feet where a mason's mark can, and a girl put her hand flat on it in the summer and it was not warm.** **No System panel describes the seam and no character links it to the valley, and the Volume 06 row of the failure table is Volume 06's and is not scheduled here and no consequence of a cut is forecast with a date by anybody in Volume 04.**

11. **The fourth route, the offer, the refusal, and the word eastern.** Item 491. **The offer is a sheet of good paper in the ordinary post from a county four hundred miles off, unopened for twelve days and opened by a keeper of records, and it gives a hand, a room, a house, four shillings the week and a clerk, and it requires the eastern paths to be closed by that man and by nobody else, and it requires a county of nine hundred and forty-one hearths to be entered by number and not by name with the number withheld from the man, and it requires that no question be asked of him about the object of the appointment by anybody at any time. The refusal is four sheets, dated, with the hour as a second thing and not merged, and it is in a bag in the ordinary post and a copy is on a shelf with a number against the office and nothing against a person.** **The second copyist refused to open it and said the day she opens the eleventh letter from that county is the day this office is a body corporate that goes through a county's post, and she wanted a line in the day-book and does not want it, and both of those are the volume's argument about who holds a record in its smallest form.** Open: the offering party is not named in Volume 04, the march is not a character, nobody is harmed, the refusal is correct, and **the word eastern is now in a document in a file and in a bag in two counties and a city that has been hearing about the eastern paths for a very long time has never been asked anything.**

12. **The intimacy, the three things, the refusal to walk up the road, and the third rule's final clause.** Item 492. **Open, and the volume's chosen intimacy is bought and is not a cure.** The breach is not repaired in a scene, an aside, a document or an apology; no apology in any form is available to anybody; neither of them has said they have been wrong; the not-askings stand at nine and this block entered no tenth and put no number on the page. **The third of the three rules on the wall of a room over a weigh-house ends in a sentence about a habit that a place does to itself when nobody is looking and it is about him and she wrote it four months before the volume opened and he knows it is about him and neither of them has ever said so and this block did not make either of them say it and did not put it near a document in a way that states it.** And she has said out loud that she is not going to walk up that road with him and has given the reason in nine sentences. **The second not-asking, a man at a gate in a valley who will not say what a word means, was not turned into a mirror of the first and no character in this block noticed that the two resemble anything.**

13. **The fever, the yard, and the price.** Item 493. **Open as a cost and closed as a week.** Seven days, broken on the seventh morning, thirty-five days before the volume's last day. A man of twenty-five held two boxes on a bench-board for seven days and wrote nothing down and said a barrow was out of true and that he had not looked at it because it is not his to look at. He ran nine errands, four of them for other people. He told a woman of about seventy where his brother was on the second day and did not say anything else, and she asked him on the fourth day what the place was called and he said it was called a valley and that he did not know the name of it and had not asked. **The word is not used in a room the two of them are in anywhere in the block, the spent sentence is not re-said, no Volume 04 scene re-teaches him an ordinary morning, and the yard is off the page for the rest of the block and that absence is a cost.** The Volume 04 close must report the estrangement as untouched and must not attempt a reconciliation and must not put the word brother into a room.

14. **The carrier of about fifty, the pass, and the 417-mile question.** Item 494. **Open, and it is a question and not a man and no batch may manufacture one.** He has been up the pass twice in eleven weeks, has a rate on about a hundred and forty miles of haulage in four counties, came up two flights of a stair with a figure footed in pence on a page and asked the bench of nine for thirty-one miles of road and was refused in eleven words over a chest with two marks in it, came down two flights again on his own on the fourth of a fever and told the bench that the man they had heard about was in the town, and on the last day of Volume 04 said out loud in a hall that he did not go up the pass, and why, and had it minuted: **a man of fifty with a waggon of his own goes up it to carry things and to be paid for carrying them, and the day he goes up it to ask is the day he has a reason to be listened to that has nothing to do with the asking.** And he is the holder of Batch 0004's fifth thread, the hand in a valise, and a waggon-book is a yard and a yard is a record and he is the person who keeps it.

15. **Nesta Garrow, 41, and the top of the pass, and four hundred miles of low country.** Item 484. **Open.** She keeps the top of the pass, and has for about two years, and nobody has come up it, and there is a trough and a barrow and four of the ninth go up it twice a year with beasts. She is the granddaughter of the woman of about forty-one who keeps the road at the County of Ash end, **is not the same person, has never met her, and does not know that the road at that other end has been shut in four lines of writing since before she was born. The road to the Marches' end of the line stays closed and nothing in Volume 04 or after may reopen it, and the six terms are not re-worded and are not quoted, and no Volume 04 character is a Marches character coming home.** Her want, said once, to a bridgekeeper and not to a stranger: **that the road be made and that nobody be able to say who made it.** And a woman of about thirty-five who has asked the man of thirty-four twice about the bank with a beck under it and has been told twice that he has not got anything, and who has said she is going to keep asking.

16. **The form under the byre, the four columns, the two hands, and the three questions.** Not mentioned once in Chapters 191 to 200. **Open, unadvanced and unscheduled.** The form is a printed Return of the Office of Quarters at Grayford, about two hundred years old, eight columns, two hands, a third column answered *four in this year* in a reckoning that valley cannot use, a fifth column answered *none appointed*, a seventh column whose rule has not been ruled across, an eighth column no more faded than the third, and nine printed words at the foot. **The three questions it opens — who put it there and when, who took four columns out of the middle of the book, and whether the two hands on it have anything to do with the hand in the third column of the ninth spread — are not answered by this block and are not scheduled by it, and a batch summary that says this block closed the form is a summary that has not read Chapter 172.** The four lines at the head of the first spread were used once, in Chapter 191, as the rule that puts eight names in eight hands into a second column, and they were not re-derived and not improved on. The two accounts of where a word came from are not joined. The two figures of the ninth generation that do not agree with each other are used in Chapter 196 and are not reconciled and neither is struck out.

17. **The mechanism, the counterweight, the tile, the bell, the hearing, the count, the four claims, the seed letter and the seed vault.** **All open and none answered by this block and none scheduled by it.** The mechanism was said once, in Chapter 164, on a bank, in the language of a valley that builds roads, and it is spent and was not restated. The counterweight of Chapter 177 is spent and was not restated. **The tile on a coping four hundred miles off, a hand's width, with a rope on it in two loops and no yoke and no headstock and no striker, and a man of sixty-four there who wanted it lifted because a lifting is a job and a taking away is a throwing away, is mentioned once in this block as an object, is not explained, its device is named by nobody, and no batch may schedule an explanation, a flashback, an origin, a maker or a deathbed.** The bell with forty-one rivets went at the fourth hour and a half on the twenty-fifth of Fallowmonth, rung by a boy of seventeen and a carpenter, and the arrangement is not anybody's and nothing in the sound of it belongs to anybody, and a Monday is the day the far end keeps and the volume's last day is a Monday and the two facts are in two books in two counties and are not joined in one sentence. The hearing stands and was not adjourned and not refused and the delegation is not sent for. The count of 2,640 is not restated. The strip of cut paper in the press weighs a shade over ten ounces and is the account of nobody and the number of persons the offer was made out to is still not on the sheet and has been asked for in a return and has not come. A bundle of nine things from a county four hundred miles off has a piece of tarred hemp through it and the tenth was opened in Harvestmonth. The letter for a jar is not answered and not delivered and no living holder of the other end is found. The three marks on the outside of a seed vault are not read and the keeper's decision is not taken and the spring was nine generations ago and is hers. The brine weights are neither mentioned nor filed. The five blanks of Volume 02 are not joined. The eighth ruled line is not looked at. The two bare inches at the right-hand edge of a list of things sent are not filled and the blank ruled column in a bench's minute is not filled. Sian Pugh's name is not spoken. **Edric Hunn is not written anywhere in Volume 04 and is not in a column and is not in a room, and `outline/series.md`'s Volume 4 escalation line and knowledge-control row remain unsatisfied and are carried forward as an open item, and no later phase may read Volume 04 as having satisfied them.**

18. **The drift, the errand of the error, and the notebook.** Item 496. **Untouched and not reconciled and not to be reconciled.** The printed warning is three weeks, in an office's hand, for two years, and it is not going to change, and the office cannot say whose figure it is, and **no character in this block uses the word *failed* about a road.** Main world: absence 192 days, the twentieth of Harvestmonth, YR 314, a Monday, not a market day; error 171 days against the printed three weeks, about nine tenths of the absence; the ratio is error over absence and never over the warning and the path interval is nowhere in the sum. **No character in Volume 04 is told any of that figure.** He is still not writing a drift figure; the two unreconciled numbers of his own count and the valley's count are on one page of a notebook in a coat and neither has been converted, and the third thing on that page is a date in a county four hundred miles off that nothing he is carrying has a road to, and he got to that page a fifth time in this block and did not write a figure on it. Main-world absence 192 days; error 171; about nine tenths.

19. **The figures, the devices and the length, and what the Volume 04 close must not re-derive.** **34,157 words as repaired by both review passes, per chapter 3306 / 3142 / 3405 / 3287 / 3550 / 3586 / 3249 / 3503 / 3964 / 3165, footing at 34,157, and the five Volume 04 columns counted on the page are 33,045 / 33,385 / 34,216 / 34,011 / 34,157, footing at 168,814 in fifty chapters, and the manuscript is 770,456 in two hundred chapters. Three sets of older figures are true of the page at the moment each was printed and all three are left where they were as the facts they were — 33,777 / 168,434 / 770,076, the writing phase's; 34,133 / 168,790 / 770,432, the first repair's; and the totals that came down four state files after Batch 0004, which do not foot by a hundred and seventy-nine words. A close that foots from any of them will foot from a block that no longer exists, and the close must count.** `---` rules **37**, one per chapter and a second in six and a third in three; bolded line-openings **95**; semicolons **0**; em-dashes **0**; straight double quotes **0**; curly apostrophes **0**; **`the whole of` 1** against Batch 0004's 3, and the first pass of this block carried it at 26, and the sole survivor is Chapter 195's *the whole of that week's work*; `about N seconds` **1**, the echo of the refusal at a door in Chapter 179; `cut` **29** and all twenty-nine ordinary and the working not among them and not named; **one System panel in the block, two plain lines, no number in it, not a list**; no Gatehouse-voice line. Filler as whole words, a baseline and not a target: **`nodded` 0, `held` 7, `wrote` 25, `stood` 42, `plain` 0, `counted` 16, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0.** **`about` 404 and runs 42 / 43 / 41 / 41 / 37 / 43 / 41 / 44 / 38 / 34, and no chapter is over forty-four.** **`nine` is 248 in 34,157 words, 7.26 per 1,000, per chapter 27 / 27 / 18 / 27 / 25 / 36 / 28 / 15 / 22 / 23, and the volume's six-block rate reads 2.87 / 4.58 / 7.31 / 11.11 / 5.40 / 7.26, so the Batch 0004 review's reversal did not hold; the residue in this block was `nine people` used as a hedge and not `about nine`, and the two ceilings the review set are held with room, `about nine + noun` 1 / 0 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 and a maximum of two against a ceiling of four, and `in nine years` maximum two against a ceiling of three. Three of the figures in this thread were wrong against the page at the time the first repair pass wrote it and are corrected here, in the Block 5 note and in continuity item 510.** **One money beat, footed in pence on the page before any total is said: 3 sixteen times over is 48, 48 thirty-one times over is 1,488, 4 twice over is 8, and 1,488 and eight is 1,496 pence, which is thirty-one marks and eightpence**, and the valley's own price of that month is eleven kitchens and the standing of a mill that has not ground and a root four households call a root, and the two are never added. Batch 0004's 3,032 and 1,920 and Chapter 190's twelve pence are not restated. **Two calendar defects were found in this block's own text and repaired — a main-world weekday spoken aloud in a valley room and a main-world weekday in a valley bank — and five broken bold spans and two curly apostrophes were found and repaired, and a per-line parity check across Chapters 191 to 200 and across the whole manuscript now returns clean.** The name budget is **one name of six and four slots unfilled, Volume 04 closing at six of six and twenty-three of twenty-four unfilled, twenty-nine of thirty slots**, and the earlier figure of *twenty-seven of twenty-four* did not foot and is not reconciled.

**The threads this block opened, and it closes three of the inherited and nothing else.** **Ten new threads are open and they are listed at 1, 2, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 13, 14 and 15 above and they are the volume's hand-off to the close.** **And the close must say plainly that Volume 04 held its threads and did not stop: it opened ten, closed three, answered none of the inherited nineteen from the Volume 03 close, none of the nineteen Batch 0001 carried forward, none of Batch 0002's, none of Batch 0003's and none of Batch 0004's, and every one of those stands.**
