## Threads the Volume 04 Batch 0005 Review Repair Pass carries into the Volume 04 close

**This block opened no thread and closed none. It is a review of a finished batch and it changed no event, no date, no figure a character speaks, no money figure, no cast name and no outline.** Eight findings, all repaired, and the record is the **Volume 04 — Batch 0005 — Review Repair Pass** entry in `state/batch-summaries.md` and continuity items 498 to 506, with items 489 and 490 annotated rather than renumbered. **The volume's threads stand exactly as the writing phase left them: ten opened, three closed, none of the inherited answered, and the close must say plainly that Volume 04 held its threads and did not stop.**

**Three threads are touched by the repair and none of them changes state.**

1. **Thread 4, the stranding and the return, is closed and stays closed.** The repair changed one fact inside it and the fact was wrong: the witness at that end is **Bram Ottery, on the bench of nine because he was elected and not because he is on a trade, paid fourpence a week out of a private box, and not the person keeping the reach**, which is Chapter 152's description of him and the job Chapters 156 and 157 gave him. The Batch 0005 first pass called him *a road warden on the roll with a vote and not an office*, which is Aren Kest's own position and would have put a second man in the protagonist's chair. **The way out is still not the stitch, the stitch is still gone, and no second mark, second line, second witness, preparation or offer exists anywhere in Chapters 191 to 200.**
2. **Thread 10, the seam and the mark, is open and stays open and is still neither explained nor joined.** The repair put the sentence about a figure and a decline and a survey and a form back in the guild's man with the levels' mouth, in Chapter 150's wording, said in the open to a yard of forty men in Chapter 198 where the first pass had it in an invented man's mouth and re-worded. **No System panel describes the seam, no character links it to the valley, the Volume 06 row is Volume 06's and is not scheduled, and no consequence of a cut is forecast with a date by anybody in Volume 04.**
3. **Thread 19, the figures and the devices, is corrected and the close must foot from the corrected column and not from the older one: 34,133 words for the block, per chapter 3306 / 3142 / 3406 / 3287 / 3550 / 3586 / 3248 / 3503 / 3962 / 3143, and 168,790 for the volume and 770,432 for the manuscript.** The 33,777 / 168,434 / 770,076 that stood here before the repair were true of the page before eight findings were fixed. **Three of this repair's own device figures were wrong against that page and the review of the repair corrected them at item 510: `the whole of` is 1 and not 2, `about` is 404 and not 414, and `about nine + noun` runs 1 / 0 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 and is nine in the block and not eleven. The word-count figures in this item are right and are superseded by item 515, which carries the block at 34,157 per chapter 3306 / 3142 / 3405 / 3287 / 3550 / 3586 / 3249 / 3503 / 3964 / 3165, the volume at 168,814 and the manuscript at 770,456.**

**And the threads the repair explicitly did not touch.** **The nine and the three households are closed by being made exact and stay closed, and none of the nine is named in prose.** **The form under the byre is not mentioned once in Chapters 191 to 200, its three questions are not answered and are not scheduled, the four lines at the head of the first spread are used once and are not re-derived, and the two figures of the ninth generation that do not agree are used and are not reconciled and neither is struck out.** **The two unreconciled numbers of his own count and the valley's count are not reconciled here and nothing schedules their reconciliation, and no page in this block converts either of them.** The mechanism is not restated, the counterweight of Chapter 177 is not restated, the refusal of Chapter 166 is not repeated, the consent of Chapters 178 to 180 is not re-asked, the tile on the coping is an object and is not explained and its device is not named, the bell is rung by other hands, the hearing is not adjourned and not refused, the number of persons the offer was made out to is still not on the sheet, the count of 2,640 is not restated, the not askings stand at nine and no tenth is entered, and the keeper's decision about the three marks is not taken. **The three reasons given at three doors in Chapter 179 are referred to and not restated, as repaired at item 503.** **`outline/series.md`'s Volume 4 escalation line and knowledge-control row remain unsatisfied, and no phase may read Batch 0003, Batch 0005 or this repair as having satisfied them.**

## Threads the Review of the Batch 0005 Review Repair Pass carries into the Volume 04 close

**This pass opened no thread and closed none. It reviewed a finished batch's repair and changed no event, no date, no figure a character speaks, no money figure, no cast name, no outline and no chapter order. Eight findings, all repaired, and the record is the **Volume 04 — Batch 0005 — Review of the Review Repair Pass** entry in `state/batch-summaries.md`, continuity items 507 to 516, and `reviews/volume-04/batch-0005.md`, which now holds the findings of both passes and the disposition of all sixteen. **The volume's threads stand exactly as the writing phase and the first repair left them: ten opened by the block, three of the inherited closed, none of the inherited answered.**

**Four threads are touched by this pass and none of them changes state.**

1. **Thread 4, the stranding and the return, is closed and stays closed, and the description of the witness is now right in every file including the volume's last paragraph.** **The first repair's own finding had survived at `chapter-0200.md` line 23, which put the man of forty-seven back into a trade and a roll that Chapter 198 denies in the same yard, and the wrong description had also been written into the Volume 04 close prompt as an instruction.** He is **Bram Ottery, on the bench of nine because he was elected and not because he is on a trade, paid fourpence a week out of a private box, the witness at that end and not the person keeping the reach**, and a later phase must not read him as a road warden in any file. **The two *road warden* lines in Chapter 199 are the terms of the refused fourth route and are correct and must not be touched either.**

2. **Thread 10, the seam and the mark, is open and stays open and is still neither explained nor joined.** No System panel describes the seam, no character links it to the valley, and the man of sixty with the levels' sentence about a decline and a survey is Chapter 150's and is said a second time in a yard of forty men and not invented for the occasion. **The one thing this pass changed inside the thread is Chapter 199 line 9, where the witness said *I have been in this trade nineteen years* while Chapter 198 said he had not been on a trade since the year before last; he now says he has stood in yards in this town nineteen years, and the nineteen years are not cut.**

3. **Thread 19, the figures and the devices, is corrected a second time and the close must foot from the second correction.** **34,157 words for the block, per chapter 3306 / 3142 / 3405 / 3287 / 3550 / 3586 / 3249 / 3503 / 3964 / 3165, and 168,814 for the volume in fifty chapters and 770,456 for the manuscript in two hundred chapters.** `nine` is 248 and 7.26 per 1,000. **`the whole of` is 1, `about` is 404 with a highest chapter of 44, and `about nine + noun` is nine in the block and not eleven.** All three sets of older figures — 33,777 / 168,434 / 770,076, 34,133 / 168,790 / 770,432 and 134,478 / 736,120 — were true of the page at the moment they were printed and are left standing as the facts they were, and **a close that prints a total must foot it on the page first, which is the whole of continuity item 145 and of the Volume 02 close's §10 item 3, and which this is now the fourth worked example of.**

4. **The `nine` per-chapter column moved and the total did not.** It is 248 in both passes and the column is now 27 / 27 / 18 / 27 / 25 / 36 / 28 / 15 / 22 / 23, against 27 / 27 / 18 / 27 / 25 / 36 / 29 / 15 / 22 / 22 before: Chapter 197 gave up one and Chapter 200 took one, both in the same pass, and a total that holds while a column moves is the clearest argument for printing the column.

5. **A thread about how this project records its own work, which is a real defect and not a formality.** **The first pass's nine findings went into a gitignored log and into a canon ledger and not into `reviews/`, so the reasoning behind nine repairs survives in a document written to be read as fact and not as argument; the second pass's seven went the same way until this file was written. `reviews/volume-04/batch-0005.md` now holds both, and `reviews/README.md` has its entry.** A second part of the same defect: **all of that work sits in one commit labelled *novel: save writer work batch-0005*, because the review phase's agent fell back from `novel-reviewer` to the writer and the runner could not tell them apart. The durable remedy is the file and not the commit label, and a later phase should look for a `reviews/` entry before it trusts a repair's reasoning to be anywhere else.**

**And what this pass explicitly did not touch.** **The nine and the three households are closed by being made exact and stay closed, and none of the nine is named in prose. The form under the byre is not mentioned once in Chapters 191 to 200 and its three questions are not answered and not scheduled. The two figures of the ninth generation that do not agree are used and not reconciled. The tile on a coping four hundred miles off is an object and is not explained. The two unreconciled numbers of his own count and the valley's count are on a page in a coat and were not reconciled. The bell is rung by other hands. The hearing is not adjourned and not refused. The number of persons the fourth route was made out to is still not on the sheet. Stage 3 is still reached by qualification in Chapters 191 to 195 and by nothing else, and the cut is still consented to and unnamed.** **The close must report all of that and must answer none of it, and a close that resolves any of it has read a summary and not the threads.**

## Threads the Volume 04 close hands to Volume 05 Batch 0001

**The full record is `outline/volumes/volume-04-close.md`, and its §5 is the state-of-the-world ledger and its §7 is the twenty-two corrections Volume 05 must not undo. This section is a reading list, not a substitute. Nothing below is closed, nothing below is answered, and nothing below was answered by the close or scheduled by the Volume 05 outline.**

1. **The path city's refusal, which is the volume's whole engine and is a thread, not a setting.** A city on a river under a standing magical dawn that has chosen to be unfindable for four hundred years, a council of eleven, a duty kept in shifts, and a refusal given nine times in four generations. **Nobody inside it will explain what a roadmark is, and one man has kept a ledger of them for most of his life and wants to be asked to stop.** Nobody has asked him. That is the volume. **It is not a rescue and no character may describe anything as one, and the city does not want to be found, explained, studied or pitied, and a reader should be able to hear all four refusals in the outline.**

2. **The ninth refusal, and it is a document rather than a scene.** A main-world office's form has a space for a figure and no space for a name, the city's answer is a count, and the count is right. **The number of refusals has gone up by one because a child of nine said no, and the city's own keeper is the one who says out loud that the city has begun filing a person as a class.** Open: what the office does with the figure. **It does not appear in the city, it never appears, and no character realises anything in a room.**

3. **The return being served on a body corporate, by nobody.** A fourth refusal in a document in four volumes, a return that names a district that is not on the office's own form, and an office that has been asked twice for the number of persons its offer was made out to and has not produced it. **The number is never printed on the offer and is printed on a return, and nobody in that office connects the two figures and a reader can.** Mara Vey has it in her register and does not open it in a chapter where he is in the room, **and she does not enter a district as a finding, because a keeper who enters a thing she means to do has made a plan.**

4. **Ilyra's line in a second column, and the column with no outcome.** The series mandates the reveal at Volume 05's midpoint: the city's citizens know that paths know about each other and that a woman crossed one in YR 301 with a boy. **The column is dated, eleven hands over four hundred years, and the keeper says there is no outcome column before he is asked, and he is not asked.** **Her survival stays intentionally unknown. Her route is Volumes 09 and 18, the Volume 06 midpoint is not pre-empted, and no Volume 05 chapter may tell him what became of her.** Thread 5 and thread 10 above are unchanged by this.

5. **The fourth route refused in writing, a second time, in a document and not a scene.** A third refusal in a document in four volumes, from a different party, on better terms, with the word *eastern* on the sheet. **A bench that cannot be asked to be his witness this year, a council that will not be told, and a child who cannot be asked twice are the volume's three refusals, and all three are correct.** Thread 1 of the initial list and the volume's second route are unchanged.

6. **The seam and the mark, still neither explained nor joined.** About nine inches in a stone course in the fourth bay of the Sedge Reach, under half an inch of lime, not moving, that a carter of thirty found and that Bram Ottery looked at once and did not measure. **Volume 05 opens in that yard and about two hundred yards of reaching work go past it four times in four chapters and nobody measures it, and that is the volume's treatment of it.** It is not a door, not a key, not a mark, and it does not move.

7. **The figures the close found wrong, and they are now the numbers.** `nine` **941** in the volume, per block **95 / 153 / 250 / 195 / 248**; `about` **1,801**; `the whole of` **1** in the fifth block and **53** in the volume; `about N seconds` **1**; `about nine + noun` **9**; `in nine years` **7**; `cut` **35** in the fifth block and all ordinary. **Volume 04 columns 33,045 / 33,385 / 34,216 / 34,011 / 34,157 = 168,814, and the manuscript 770,456.** **Every older figure in this file is left standing as the fact it was, and a Batch 0001 writer foots from the page and not from here.**

8. **The market rule, which two files in this repository state wrong in opposite directions.** The county keeps **two market days a week, Saturday and Thursday** — forty-three in Volume 05's hundred and fifty-one days, sixty-nine in Volume 04's two hundred and forty. `bible/world.md`'s fourth-and-ninth line is true of one month and not the rule; continuity item 153's Saturday-and-Thursday line is the rule and is stated as a description. **Reconcile them in a later phase, not in a chapter.** Thread 19 above is the same defect in a different register: figures carried instead of counted.

9. **The Culling Order, and the fact that it is a thread about naming.** The phrase is spent twice in two hundred chapters and is not spoken until a volume that puts a member to it. **The close ruled that Volume 05 is not that volume and the outline follows, and `outline/series.md`'s Volume 5 escalation line is therefore not satisfied in full and a later phase may not read the outline as having satisfied it.** The pressure arrives without a member, in a form. **The first named member on a page is Volume 06's, and the escalation line's honest debt is recorded in `outline/volume-05.md` §16 item 1 and not absorbed.**

10. **The antagonist's name, which is now in neither new document.** It was written into the close and removed from every occurrence, and the outline does not contain it, and **`outline/series.md`'s Volume 4 knowledge-control row stays unsatisfied because of it.** Thread 89 and the Volume 02 close's §7.1 hold the second severance for Chapter 894. **A phase that eventually writes the name checks that thread and the separate-sheet rule and joins no two halves in one sentence before Chapter 894.** This is a thread about the project's own record and it is not a plot thread, and it is recorded because a defect that is inherited silently becomes a canon with a footnote.

11. **Everything the close refused to answer, restated so a Batch 0001 writer cannot answer it by accident.** The form's three questions. The two unreconciled numbers on a page in a coat. The keeper's decision about the three marks on the outside of a seed vault, and the nine bushels of barley in a four-foot aisle, and the four hundred miles of shut road. The subscription sentence. The tile on a coping and its device and its maker. The marginal instruction, which may be printed, quoted in a return, cited in a file and refused about in a gateway, and which **Sabin Dain may not explain**. The mechanism. The roadmark. Why the standing light does not turn. The seam. The bell. **The hearing entered for the ninth of Rainmonth of the year following was heard with nobody in it, was not adjourned and not refused, and the delegation is still not sent for, and it now has a companion file because a return has gone out naming a district the delegation was for.**

12. **A thread about this project's own record, which is the fifth time it has been raised and the first time it is raised by a phase that created a document rather than a repair.** **The close called itself the third document of its kind and it is the fourth, and the fault is on its own face and is recorded there.** Two audited weekday chains in this project disagree by one day and the Volume 05 outline anchors on the nearest anchor rather than resolving it. **A defect that is recorded is a fact about the book, and this project has now been saved four times by that sentence and hurt by the four times before it.**

## Threads Volume 05 Batch 0001 carries into Batch 0002

**The full record is `state/continuity.md` items 537 to 565 and the Batch 0001 entry in `state/batch-summaries.md`, and the per-chapter entries and the Block 1 note in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. This section is a reading list, not a substitute. Nothing below is closed, nothing below is answered, and nothing below was answered by the close of Volume 04 or by this block.** **The twelve threads the Volume 04 close handed to Volume 05 Batch 0001, and every thread each of Batches 0001 to 0005 of Volume 04 carried forward, all stand, and this block closed none of them and answered none of them.** Batch 0002 writes Chapters 211 to 220, which is Block 2, which is the first ten chapters inside the city, and the block's own question is in thread 1 below.

1. **The seventh line of a page of foolscap in a coat, which says *a person*, and which was looked at twice and was not finished.** Chapter 208, the seventh of Embermonth. It is the volume's live question and it is the thing Batch 0002 opens on, **because the block's seventh child is the person at that line and the child is nine and has never been asked anything and cannot be filed.** It is also the volume's answer in a form nobody has said out loud yet: a person can be asked a second time and a column cannot, and this block does not put that in anybody's mouth, and `outline/volume-05.md` §8 Block 2 places it in the city in about eleven seconds from a person with no stake in the city. **Held open: whether the child is asked a second time, and by whom, and what the second asking is, and whether anybody in that city is ever told that the first asking was a child.** And held open underneath it: **a road is opened at the far end by whoever is standing at the far end, and the man of thirty-two worked that out standing up at the fifth hour of a Sunday and stepped back off the sill, and there is no man in that county who can say what happened and nobody has written it down.**

2. **The page, and the word on it, and the fact that four people in Merefen know the word and nobody in that county has said it aloud.** A page torn out of something, folded in three, dated the fourteenth of Harvestmonth, YR 314, thirty-five days old when it was handed over. **The word is the name of a place, and the same word was spoken out loud in a stove room in a provincial town on the fourth of Embermonth by a man of sixty-one with a satchel, and it went onto a leaf of a working book and onto a line with no head on a form, and the form has no column for a place, and the form is the only paper that left that building.** Open: what the child is, where it came from, who sent it, why a child is the city's post, and why nobody in a town of four thousand people asked it a question in nine days. **Nobody in Merefen knows the city's name, nobody in Merefen knows a child came from four hundred miles off, and nobody in Merefen learns what a sample is of — and that last fact is fixed at continuity item 281's successor for this block: `state/current.md`'s knowledge table for the public is a plan, and this block delivers it.**

3. **The mark, three of them now, and the seam, and none of them joined.** The line and a bar and a figure in the coping of the first bay, since the sixth of Goatmonth, YR 313. The line and a bar and a figure in the outside face of the parapet of the fourth bay, cut on the twenty-fifth of Fallowmonth, YR 314, in daylight in front of about twenty men, with the reason given in four sentences before anybody asked. **And a line and a bar and a figure on the left hand of a child of nine who came up four hundred yards of lane four times in two days and put a flat hand on a cold sill.** The seam is under about half an inch of lime in the third course of the fourth bay, has not moved, was mentioned once in this block, is not measured, and is not joined to any of the three. **Held open: whether the child's mark is the same mark, and nobody in the block has said that it is and nobody has said that it is not, and the volume does not join them, and a Batch 0002 writer may not join them either.** The figure in both parapet and coping cannot be read at thirty feet by anybody in that county and a mason's mark can be.

4. **Two names in a column that has a head, and a form with a sixth line that does not.** Osmund Cusk, 61, in the third column of a return of the Office of Provincial Registration, filled in by a trader in a stove room on the fourth of Embermonth, YR 314, and copied onto a leaf of a woman's own hand because she was not going to trust the column, and the leaf is in a book of working papers on a second shelf and has been looked at by three people in nine weeks. **Nesh Karrow, 28, in the same column, and described in a trader's own mouth: a woman at the edge of that place who carries, four years, refused at a gate, politely, who said she is not refused because she is nobody and is refused because of what she is carrying.** Open: what either of them keeps and what either of them is refused, and **Batch 0002 must give them their pages, must not restate this description, and must not improve on it.** Cusk is described in the outline as a man who has wanted, for most of his working life, to be asked to stop keeping a ledger, and who says so once in a yard in about nine seconds. Nothing in this block says what the ledger is.

5. **A jar a hand's width and a half, in a lock-up in a yard in a provincial town, and a trader's figure of a hundred and forty marks for it that he has neither taken nor refused, and a house in a port that buys everything anybody brings it and does not know what is in it.** Open: what the jar is, who else wants it, and **that nobody in this block is told what a sample of anything is of and that no character in this block describes the sample, the light or the city as anything but a thing a man came back with.** Also open: whether the lock-up and the man paid to sit on it is the arrangement that lets a jar move at all, which is a trade fact and not a moral and is the only reason the sample is still in the world.

6. **The office that asks for a figure of persons twice a year and has printed one in eleven years, and whose returns carry a figure of obligations, and whose form has no column for a place.** Open, and this is the volume's engine and it is a document and not a scene: **a sheet of good paper in a file for five months that nobody in that office has read; a return asked for that did not come; a precedent in good paper that went to nine counties and got eight figures and one empty space with four lines beside it; and a second asking that went out in a bag on the seventh of Embermonth, YR 314, with a ruled space for a figure of persons and nowhere on the sheet for the name of a person.** Held open: **what the office does with the figure, and the fact that the office has a habit of counting and not a motive, and that the count of obligations is the only kind of paper a person cannot refuse.** The four sheets in the tray are the same instrument as the second asking and the office has not connected them and this block has not and neither may Batch 0002, **and the office must not learn of the Bellweather Claims Archive in this volume.**

7. **A bench of nine that has refused a road twice in one sitting, and the first refusal is a refusal to keep and the second is a refusal to witness.** Open, and it is the first time in nineteen years a bench has refused to be a witness rather than to be a keeper, and the reason is in a clerk's hand with the politeness entered. **Also open: the seventh asking itself, which is on a page under that stair, and the fifth asking that was not put, and the six askings and four yeses, and the fact that the reeve has asked the minute to record that two refusals in nineteen years are the two refusals.** **A batch in Block 5 must not re-use the seventh asking, must bring the bench its eighth thing, and must not have the eleventh words be a variation of these two.** The chest has two marks in it and nothing in this block is added to them.

8. **A man of forty-seven who has voted once, who has said out loud in a room that he is not a road warden, and who has been put at the end of three crossings in a year and is the only person in that county anybody can be put at the end of a thing with.** Open, and he is unpaid for all three except fourpence a week out of his own box, and he is the far end of the two mark-making events in the case and he was a witness at the third. **Held open: whether he is ever asked to hold anything, and the fact that he is one of the nine of a bench that has just refused twice, and that a man who votes once and then says he is not a road warden has put himself in a position nobody in that hall can use.** A writer in Block 2 or 4 who wants the witness mechanic satisfied in the city must not satisfy it with him, because he is four hundred miles off and the road he is on is closed from the other end.

9. **A Sunday, a bare lane, a market that is twice a week, and a road that goes out on the one day the county is neither working nor trading.** Open as a fact about the county and not as a date: **that a Saturday is the one day anybody in that county can be asked a thing, that the bench sits on Saturdays, and that a man who is going to be somewhere in four days is not going to be asked anything.** When the return comes on the eighth of Hearthmonth, YR 315, a Saturday, and a market morning, the same rule applies to the fever, and **the seven days of the fever are in a week that contains a Saturday on which the bench sits and a market on which about four hundred people are in the street**, and no batch may arrange for a room to be refused on a Sunday.

10. **A form with an empty space in it going two hundred miles in a bag on the second of Embermonth, YR 314, and four lines beside it saying that an office which will not print a number of persons cannot show that any person has not been instructed.** Open, and it is the first refusal in a document in this volume and it is correct, and it is in the ordinary post, **and nobody in the provincial town has connected it to anything and nobody in Merefen will ever be told what was in the tray.** Held open: whether the office ever learns that a body corporate in a fen county exists, which is a later volume's discovery in a file and is not a scene, and the fact that the county's own return is the only piece of paper in the world that this office cannot answer, because the answer to it would be a figure.

11. **The marginal instruction, the mechanism, the roadmark, why the standing light does not turn, the bell, the tile, the seam, the form under the byre, the two unreconciled numbers, the keeper's three marks, the nine bushels, the four hundred miles of shut road, the subscription sentence, the hearing, and the count of 2,640: all open, all untouched by this block, and none scheduled by it.** **The tile on the coping is not mentioned once in Chapters 201 to 210 and its device is not named and no explanation, flashback, origin, maker or deathbed is scheduled.** The hearing is neither adjourned nor refused and the delegation is not sent for. The not askings stand at nine and this block entered no tenth. **The breach is open and no apology in any form is available to anybody and none is made, and Mara Vey's sentence from the end of Volume 04 is not re-opened, re-told, shortened or improved on, and it is not said in this block.** The brothers are not healed, the word is not used, and Toban's yard is off the page for all ten chapters.

12. **The Culling Order, and the fact that this block does not satisfy `outline/series.md`'s Volume 5 escalation line and may not be read as satisfying it.** **What arrived instead of a member is a form, a return, an office's habit of asking for a number, a trader's jar, a count of obligations in a press, and a name on a line with no head.** The phrase is not spoken and no member is named and the phrase is not going to be spoken until a volume that puts a member to it, **and the first named member of the Order on a page is Volume 06's.** The antagonist's name is not written anywhere in Chapters 201 to 210 and the separate-sheet rule holds, and thread 89 and the Volume 02 close's §7.1 still hold the second severance for Chapter 894.

13. **A Stage 3 that is held and not advanced, and a panel that has been in the book four times in four years and has been written down once.** Held open, and a Batch 0002 writer may not make a lineage reading in Block 2, **because the outline places it in Block 3 after the reveal and not before**, and no Batch 0002 chapter may cut, may open a fourth path, may worsen the limp, and may not have a character say *you are slower now* or find it out for the first time. The tax the volume spends instead is the standing one: **a path left open always costs something, and it is that he cannot stay interested in a conversation for its whole length, and in a city where recognition is the trade that is not a symptom and it is a way of being unable to be given a cup without losing the thread.**

14. **A defect of this project's own record, and the second one in five volumes that a count could not see.** A blanket device substitution in this block broke about forty sentences and was found by reading the changed lines and not by any device count, and the finding is recorded at continuity item 561 and in the batch entry at deviation 23. **The standing rule for Batch 0002: a device repair in this project must be done by reading every line it touches, and a per-line bold parity check must be run across the whole of the repository before the block is recorded done, and a duplicate-paragraph sweep must be run across the new chapters, and both checks must be recorded as having been run rather than as having come back clean.** Both were run on this block and both return zero and clean, and that is the second consecutive block in which they were run rather than recorded.

## Threads the Volume 05 Batch 0001 Review Repair Pass carries into Batch 0002

**This pass opened no thread and closed none. It is a review of a finished batch and it changed no event, no thread's state, no character's want, no money figure, no cast name, no outline and no planned plot.** Six findings, five applied and one recorded, and the record is the **Volume 05 — Batch 0001 — Review Repair Pass** entry in `state/batch-summaries.md` and continuity items **564 and 565**. **Fourteen threads were inherited by Batch 0002 from Batch 0001 and all fourteen stand exactly as they stood, and every one of them is still open, and none of them is answered.**

**Two threads are touched by the repair and neither of them changes state.**

1. **Thread 6, the office and its figure of persons, is open and stays open, and one date inside it is corrected.** The second asking went out in a bag **on the seventh of Embermonth, YR 314, a market Saturday, at about the fourth hour**, and not on the eighth, and the eighth is the crossing out and nothing in Chapters 205 to 207 is on it. **Everything else in that thread stands word for word: the sheet of good paper in a file for five months that nobody has read, the return asked for that did not come, the precedent that went to nine counties and got eight figures and one empty space with four lines beside it, the ruled space for a figure of persons, and the fact that nowhere on the sheet, in any column or any line, is there a space for the name of a person.** The office still has not learned of the Bellweather Claims Archive, still has not connected the four sheets in the tray to anything, and must not in this volume, and the count of obligations is still the only kind of paper a person cannot refuse. **Held open, exactly as before: what the office does with the figure, and the fact that it has a habit of counting and not a motive.**
2. **Thread 14, the record's own defects, gains a second instance and keeps its rule.** The blanket device substitution that broke about forty sentences and was found by reading the changed lines is at continuity item 561. **The date-chain defect found in Chapter 207 is the same class and is at item 564, and five of the six findings in this repair pass were invisible to every count in the ledger, because a date and a weekday were not on the sweep at all.** The rule is unchanged and it is now two rules: **a device repair in this project must be done by reading every line it touches, and a block that prints a chain on a page must check that chain against `outline/volume-05.md` §17 by counting and must check every weekday it prints against the day it names, before the block is recorded done, and must say in its state entry that it did.**

**And the threads this repair explicitly did not touch.** **Thread 1, the seventh line that says *a person*, is open and unaltered and is still the block's own question. Thread 2, the page and the word on it, is open, and the word was spoken aloud in that room on the fourth of Embermonth and not on the seventh, and the repair did not move it. Thread 3, three marks and a seam and none of them joined, is open and no mark was touched. Threads 4 and 5, the two names in a column and the jar in a lock-up, are open and Batch 0002 still owes both names a page. Threads 7, 8 and 9, the bench, the man of forty-seven and the county's week, are open. Thread 10, the form with an empty space in it going two hundred miles, is open. Thread 11, everything this block did not find, is open and none of it was looked at. Thread 12, the Culling Order, is open and Volume 05 still does not satisfy the series' escalation line. Thread 13, the Stage 3 held and the four panels, is open and the lineage reading is still Block 3's.** Nothing in this pass entered a tenth not asking, healed the brothers, put the word *brother* into a room, answered a question on the form under the byre, or named anybody.

## Threads Volume 05 Batch 0002 carries into Batch 0003

**The full record is `state/continuity.md` items 566 to 584, the `## Batch 0002 — Volume 05, Chapters 211–220, "The City Under the Light"` entry in `state/batch-summaries.md`, and the per-chapter entries and Block 2 note in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. This section is a reading list, not a substitute. Nothing below is closed, nothing below is answered, and nothing below was answered by the Volume 04 close or by Volume 05 Batch 0001 or by this block.** **The fourteen threads Batch 0001 carried into Batch 0002 all stand exactly as they stood, and this block closed none of them and answered none of them.** Batch 0003 writes Chapters 221 to 230, which is Block 3, which is *The Count*, and its own question is thread 9 below.

1. **The seventh line of a page of foolscap says *a person*, it has been looked at twice and it is not finished, and at the end of Chapter 220 the man who wrote it has had four words ready for thirteen days, has not asked them, and did not ask the child a second question when the city asked it and the child said no.** Held open and **not closed by this block: whether the child is ever asked a second time, and by whom, and what the second asking is.** Two things are now on the page and are new: **the seventh line has not been looked at since the ninth day, and the seventh line and the four words are about the same thing and he has not put them together and would not be able to say how they go together.** The block did not answer the seventh line from the city's side and did not make a narrator agree with it.

2. **The ninth refusal, and the refusal arithmetic, which is now fixed on the page and in continuity item 571 and which Batch 0003 inherits rather than derives.** Eight refusals in the city's book before Chapter 215, all given by eleven people in a room; the form in Chapter 215 is the eighth; the ninth asking is made by Crane in a yard in Chapter 220 and the ninth refusal is given by a child of nine. **Crane said it once, in about ninety words, in trade terms: a man can be asked twice and a column cannot be asked anything at all; eight of the nine were given by eleven people in a room and a room is a column; the ninth was given by a person; it is the only one of the nine he has never been able to put on a page.** **The count in the book did not move in this block, nobody looked, and about four hundred people in that city did not know and will not.** Held open: **why the count did not move, and what the count in the city is now, and the difference between the two numbers, and the fact that nobody in the city has worked out that there are two numbers.** That is the block's whole question and it is Block 0003's to state.

3. **Cusk's second column, refused in four words in Chapter 218, not turned round, and not on the page.** The head of it is one plain word meaning **carried** and is not a trade and is not a job, and there has never been a name in either column in four hundred years. **Held open, and it is the volume's series-mandated midpoint: the second column is a column of dated hands and the second column's second thing is a route, and the routes are different and they are all this valley, and eleven of them over four hundred years.** Batch 0001's thread 1 for the Volume 04 close and thread 4 of the close's twelve both stand. **The reveal is not anticipated in this block by anybody, including the man of thirty-two, and Cusk says in nineteen words that the head of the column is what we do to a thing and not what the thing is, and there is a scratch on the second leaf about the width of a thumbnail that is a thumb and is not a mark.**

4. **The city's own document that is about the outside, and the one that cannot be shown, and the fact that nobody outside the city knows why the city says no.** Crane's book, under a board, and the reason it cannot be shown is that showing it is asking, and that reason is correct, and **the effect is that the city has no record of its own reasons that anyone outside it has ever seen, and the city does not know that this is a fact about itself.** This is the volume's best structural echo and it is not explained as an echo anywhere. Held open: **Block 0003's form, which arrives with a space for a figure and no space for a name, and the city's answer to it in the ordinary way, which is a figure, and the figure being right.**

5. **The seventh child's mark, and the reason the city does not name its post, and the fact that the child is not named in any of the ten chapters.** A name in a book in this city is a person somebody can be shown; a name in about four hundred heads is not; there is one person who goes out of the gate. **Held open and not closed: the child's mark is not said to be the same as either mark in Merefen, and Aren's own palm is told in nine words that it is not one of theirs, and Cusk says there is no same, and no chapter of this block joins the seam to any mark or the marks to each other.** Block 0003 uses the child and does not name it.

6. **The lamp, the ladder, the shifts, and the roster nobody has asked to see.** The light does not turn and is called a lamp and the word is not improved on in nine chapters. A ladder goes up the east wall of the glass works at the second hour and the ninth hour and there are two men on it and one of them is about forty. **The man of fifty-two on the third shift who keeps the roster for nineteen years is not in this block, is not described, is not counted, and is Batch 0003's one new name. Nobody in Chapter 220's city was told what a roster is for and nobody asked to see the paper.** Held open: the reading that shows a roster and a shift and a man, and that the reading is Block 0003's and comes after the reveal and not before.

7. **The form of eleven words, twice, and the reason a figure of persons is the only kind of paper that cannot be refused.** **We do not give work to a person we cannot name.** A refusal that costs money is not a price and a man who offered for the paper would have turned it into a sale. A penny a name at a corner of the market street and a man of thirty-two who did not buy one. **Held open: what an office's form with a space for a figure and no space for a name does when a city that cannot be written to is handed one**, which is Block 0003's engine, and **the ordinary way this city answers a form is with a figure, and the figure is right, and it is the only piece of paper about the city that anybody outside it will ever hold.**

8. **Nesh Karrow, 28, who has a page now, and the thing nobody in that city has ever said to her.** *I want it to be a letter.* She has been carrying for four years, four days a week, and turned back at three gates, and **nobody in this city has ever said that the carrying is the job and not the failing, and it is not said in this block and it is held for a later block.** She has said she would like to go on carrying and that the arrangement has been the arrangement for four hundred years and she did not make it. Held open: her page in Block 4, which the cast plan says Block 4 is carried by, and the volume's answer to her.

9. **The office that asks for a figure of persons twice a year and has printed one in eleven years, and its form with a space for a figure and nowhere at all for a name, and the second asking that went out in a bag on the seventh of Embermonth, YR 314.** **Nothing of it is on the page in this block and no character in this block is in the same county as any of it and nobody joins anything.** Held open and unchanged: what the office does with a figure, the fact that it has a habit of counting and not a motive, and the fact that it must not learn of the Bellweather Claims Archive in this volume. **Block 0003 is where a form of that series reaches the city and the city's answer goes out in a figure, and the office still does not learn anything.**

10. **The jar a hand's width and a half in a lock-up in a yard in a provincial town, and a hundred and forty marks for it that has been neither taken nor refused, and a house in a port that buys everything anybody brings it.** **Crane refers to it once, in Chapter 215, in his own mouth, as a thing a man of fifty carried up a pass and was told to take away and came back twice, and says that if that man had been in that yard he would have been given a form and would have taken it away and come back twice and been right to. The sample is not described, the light is not described, the city is not described, and the trader's name does not appear in this block.** Held open unchanged.

11. **The man of thirty-two, refused at a gate, fed and not paid, and the safest person in a city of about nine thousand people, and nobody in that city has asked him anything in nineteen days.** A man of thirty who sells names for a penny; a gate that is a machine for letting liars through; a man of fifty who pays in bread because a man who is paid is a man somebody has had to write somewhere. **He asked four questions in nineteen days and got four straight answers in nine words each, and the fifth is the one nobody in that city would have had to stop a person for, and he has not asked it, and about four people have noticed and none of them has asked him.** Held open: what the main world does with a man who comes back after ninety days, and the fever, and the return, and the anchor.

12. **The brothers, the breach, the tenth not-asking, and Toban's yard, and all four are off the page for all ten chapters of this block.** Mara Vey is not in this block and her sentence from the end of Volume 04 is not re-opened, re-told, shortened or improved on, and nobody asks him about the morning and he does not offer it. **The not askings stand at nine and this block entered no tenth and put no number on the page.** The word *brother* is not used in a room the two of them are in because they are not in a room together. **Toban's yard is off the page for twenty chapters of this volume and that absence is the volume's second-largest cost after the city, and it is recorded rather than passed over.** Held open for Block 5 and after.

13. **The Stage 3 tax, the panel, and the lineage reading, and the two things Block 0003 must not do before the reveal.** Held: three paths, a permanent limp, a road-marked left palm, one object under a pound, a seam that has not moved, no cut, no fourth path, no worsened limp, and **the tax on the page as an ordinary working day, which is that he cannot stay interested in a conversation for its whole length and in a city where recognition is the trade arrives as a way of being unable to be given a cup without losing the thread.** One panel in the block, in Chapter 214, two plain lines, no number. **The lineage reading is Block 0003's, is used once, and comes after the reveal and not before, and this block did not make it and did not schedule it.**

14. **A defect of this project's own record, and the third block in a row in which the checks were run rather than recorded, and the standing rule this block lived by.** A scripted device pass over ten files took `about N seconds` from forty to two and in doing so left seven sentences with a conjunction missing and five narrator's leaks of this book's own structure on the page, **and every one of the twelve was found by reading a file and not by counting anything**, at continuity item 584. **The rule stands: a device repair in this project must be done by reading every line it touches, and a per-line bold parity check is necessary and not sufficient, because three bold spans in this block were left open by a repair of the bold span and still passed the parity check, since each had an even number of markers with one span unclosed.** A per-line bold parity check across all two hundred and twenty chapter files returns zero, a duplicate-paragraph sweep across the ten new chapters returns clean, and both were run and both are recorded as having been run, **which is the third consecutive block in which they were run and the sixth in which the sentence has had to be written at all.**

15. **A defect in a source document, found by this block and not absorbed, and it is the one this block is obliged to hand on.** `outline/volume-05.md` §8 Block 2's card, §12's row and §9 say three different things about who gives the ninth refusal and whether the count moves, **and this block's continuity item 571 resolves the arithmetic in a way that is self-consistent on the page and records that it is a resolution and not a quotation of the outline, and Block 0003 inherits a stated position rather than an ambiguity.** The same item records that the volume's *three months* in the city is a main-world conversion made once by a person in a notebook and that this block puts it in one man's mouth in Chapter 211 and performs it nowhere.

16. **And the standing debt, untouched and carried forward a seventh time.** **`outline/series.md`'s Volume 5 escalation line is not satisfied in full and this block is not to be read as satisfying it; the pressure arrives without a member, in a form and a market and a penny a name, and the first named member of the Order on a page is Volume 06's. The antagonist's name is not written anywhere in Chapters 211 to 220, and the separate-sheet rule holds, and thread 89 and the Volume 02 close's §7.1 still hold the second severance for Chapter 894.** Nothing in this block joined any two halves of anything and nothing in it is going to.

## Threads Volume 05 Batch 0003 carries into Batch 0004

**The full record is `state/continuity.md` items 586 to 603, the `## Batch 0003 — Volume 05, Chapters 221–230, "The Count"` entry in `state/batch-summaries.md` and the repair record appended to it, and the per-chapter entries and Block 3 note and the repair note in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. This section is a reading list, not a substitute. Nothing below is closed, nothing below is answered, and nothing below was answered by the Volume 04 close or by Volume 05 Batch 0001 or Batch 0002 or by this block.** **The sixteen threads Batch 0002 carried into Batch 0003 all stand exactly as they stood and this block closed none of them and answered none of them**, and the block opened fourteen of its own. **The section is seventeen items and the seventeenth is the Batch 0003 review repair pass, added by that pass and closing nothing.** Batch 0004 writes Chapters 231 to 240, which is Block 4 and is *The Road That Is Not Made*, and its own question is thread 3 below.

1. **The figure that has left the city, twice, and the three numbers, and the fact that nobody in the city has compared any of them.** A book under a board in a bindle yard has **eight** lines in it. A man of sixty-six's head has **nine**, because the ninth is the child's and it cannot go on a page. A printed sheet of the office's series went out of the city on the twenty-fourth with **four nines** in it, four times the same figure, and it is the only piece of paper about this city that anybody outside it will ever hold. A child of nine years old was asked the same question twice in the same yard by the same man and said no twice, and a second identical sheet came round on the twenty-ninth and went out with **four tens** in it. **The eight, the nine and the ten are on three pieces of paper in three places and two hundred miles apart and nobody has put any of them beside any other, and the only one of the three that is inside this city is the one that is wrong.** Held open: **what the office does with a figure, and whether anybody in a room two hundred miles off ever compares two returns from one district, and the man of forty-six's own prediction that nobody in a room asks why they are different, which is on the page and is not a narrator's verdict and is not contradicted by anything.**

2. **A man of about forty-six with a satchel, a list of about four hundred places, two sentences on the back of that list about four inches apart, and a name on the last line of it that nobody in this city has ever heard.** He is not an agent and not a member of anything and he is the dullest possible carrier of a printed thing, and he has been walking the same round for eight years. **He has now said four things in the tone of a man saying a price in nine days and all four are the truest things anybody in that city has heard from outside it in four hundred years, and the two sentences on the back of the list are the only part of this anybody outside the city can act on, and he has said he will not be able to explain them to anybody in about a year and has not stopped writing them down.** Held open: **whether the man of forty-six comes back, and whether the name on the last line of his list ever gets said out loud in a city, and nothing in this block put the word on a page.**

3. **The volume's remaining chain and Block 0004's own question, which is a message system the city builds and he is not allowed to help with.** **Days thirty-one to sixty of ninety are ahead, and the eleventh and twelfth lines of the roadmark ledger have not been read, and the first of the eleven has not been read, and the man of thirty-two with a limp works out on the twenty-fifth day that eleven roads in four hundred years is a little more than one every thirty years and that a city that had a road out of it every thirty years is not a city anybody could find, and that nobody in that city has ever asked how a road out and back is made.** Block 0004 is *The Road That Is Not Made*: the message system, the fourth asking refused in a form, the power he declines, the fourth route refused in writing for the second time in a document and not a scene, and the return through a living anchor who was nine and is not named. **The child was asked a second time in this block and said no and is going out through a gate on the thirtieth, and the return is made through that child and nobody in this city knows what the child is for and four hundred people in it do.**

4. **Cusk's second column, turned round on the twenty-fifth day, and what is in it, and what is still not in it.** **The column is in two parts: eleven hands and eleven roads, over four hundred years, and the routes are different and they are all out of this valley and not one of the eleven names a place anybody in that city can see from where they are standing.** Held open: **the first of the eleven, which was not read on the page and which the man of thirty-two with a limp is one day of walking away from on the thirtieth; and the fact that nobody in that city has ever asked how a road out and back is made, which is the block's live question and is the same question as thread 6; and the fact that a man of thirty-two now knows that eleven is not an accident and has been told why twice by two men in two yards who have never met.**

5. **The eleventh line: two marks, a woman's and a boy's, about a year between the going out and the coming back, fourteen years ago, no names, and no outcome column.** **A man of sixty-one said before he was asked that there is no column for what became of anybody and there is not going to be one, and a man of thirty-two with a limp did not ask, and the not asking was four seconds long, and a page of foolscap in his coat has a seventh line that says a person and was looked at on the twenty-third and on the twenty-ninth and was finished on neither.** Held open: **what became of her, which nobody in this block tells him and which is intentionally unknown; that her route is Volumes 09 and 18; and that the Volume 06 midpoint is not pre-empted by anything in this block.** **The second mark on the line is a boy's who would have been about nine fourteen years ago and is not the child of nine, and no chapter joins the two, and a man of sixty-one's Chapter 218 sentence that there is no same stands and was not re-derived.**

6. **The city's duty, its roster, and the man on the third shift, and the finding he said out loud on the thirtieth.** **A man of fifty-two has been on the third shift for nineteen years and his name is on a paper in a drawer and about four hundred people in that city could be asked what the third shift is and not one of them would be able to say, and he said that once to a stranger and then said the other thing, which is that he does not want to die on it.** He is good at the wall and not at glass, and he is bored and then he is not. **He said the volume's finding in a working yard in about nineteen words and the mechanic in his own words once, and both were said while he was working, and nobody improved on either and a man of thirty-four turned a mould and said nothing.** Held open: **the roster itself, which nobody has asked to see and which is not on the page in this block; the drawer; the third of the six; the four hundred years of eleven people choosing to pay a cost every day instead of once, which is on the page once in a lineage reading and is not explained; and the fact that the city has begun filing a person as a class in answer to a form and knows it and has not decided what to do about it and will not.**

7. **The lineage reading, made once, at the end of the twenty-seventh day, in a lane, with a hand on a wall.** **It produced a list, a column, shifts, a man of fifty-two on the third shift for nineteen years, a decision four hundred years old to pay a cost every day instead of once, and one fact about a woman and a boy in a column headed by a word meaning carried. It produced no door, no consent and no opening, and nobody in the city is told any of it and nobody is going to be.** Held open: **the residue, which is that he cannot stop seeing the eleventh of the eleven lines, which is on the page on the twenty-eighth and on the thirtieth and in a glass works where nine men are on a batch and he read the yard as a shift column and a man of fifty-two noticed him do it; and the two unreconciled figures in it, four seconds of not asking on the twenty-sixth and nine minutes of writing on the twenty-seventh, which are in the same notebook and have not been added together and are not going to be; and the panel, which he has not written down and which is one of four in his notebook in four years and of which he has written one.**

8. **A printed form of an office's series, the second one of the same series with the object column empty, and a refusal to make a document that cost nothing and produced nothing.** **The blank one went back in the satchel on the twenty-seventh and the man who asked for it said in a yard that the refusal was worth nothing and that the first figure was already gone and that not filling this one in does not take that one back, and that the room will still have the other one.** Held open: **what is on the other one, and the case it is in, and the fact that the four sheets in a tray by a stove in a main-world room are the same instrument and the office has not connected them to anything and this block does not and may not, and that the office must not learn of the Bellweather Claims Archive in this volume.** The marginal instruction is on the reverse of both sheets and is not printed in this block and is not explained and is refused about in one sentence by a man of sixty-six.

9. **The gate, the four words, the man of fifty-five in the hut, and the two times a bundle went out through three bars without them.** **A gate is a machine for letting liars through and it is one man and three bars and it has been for four hundred years, and a man of fifty-five has never once been told what his four words are for and has never wanted to know, and he did not say them on the twenty-ninth and did not say them on the thirtieth, and about four people in that hut have worked out that a bundle is a bundle and nobody has said so to him.** Held open: **whether the figure comes back a third time, and the fact that a city whose only outbound instrument is a mark in a child's hand has now twice sent a figure out inside a bale of cord and reed, and that nobody in the city knows either.**

10. **A refusal count against a figure of persons, held on the page and not only in the state files.** **A man of sixty-six said it once, in about eleven words, twice, to a yard and not to the sheet: that is a count of things this city has said no to and it is not how many of us there are.** The two prices of the same thing are never added and a refusal that costs money is not a price and the form costs nothing. Held open: **the volume's own instance, which is the office's answer to a district and Block 4 and Block 5's, and which is not this block's and must not be pre-empted, and the fact that the eight, the nine and the ten are never added to each other and never added to a figure of persons and that the block says so on the page twice.**

11. **A man of thirty-two with a limp, a stick, a road-marked left palm, a page with an unfinished seventh line that says *a person*, and eleven lines in a notebook, and a leg that is the same leg and is slower, and a body that is in no book and has been fed and not paid for thirty days and is the safest person in a city of about nine thousand people.** **On the thirtieth a man of fifty-two told him in a working yard that he is the same as a line in a form and that it is not kindness, and he could not answer it and did not try and has not written it down and is not going to.** Held open: **what a man does with a fact he cannot use, and the seventh line, which was looked at twice in this block and finished neither time, and the four words that have been ready since the sixth day of a count he does not have and have now been ready for twenty-four days, and the fact that he has watched a person be asked a second time and say no and did not ask it himself, twice.**

12. **The trades of the city, all of them still working and none of them improved: a man of thirty-four with lime who has said nine sentences in twenty-five days and every one of them about work; a woman of about thirty with a pail who has said the whole of the post once to a stranger and the names of two of the eleven twice; a man of thirty with a barrow and a mule who says a city in nine words on a hill; a man of thirty-four who has put a piece of wood under a stranger's feet for five days and never said why; a boy of fourteen who will tell a stranger where the paper is made and will not shake his hand; a man of thirty-four who can tell whether a bank is square.** **Nesh Karrow is not in this block and her want is not restated and nobody in that city has said to her that the carrying is the job and not the failing, and this is the second consecutive block that has not said it.**

13. **The boy of fourteen and the fact that a line in a book is the same age as he is, and that a city of about nine thousand people keeps a count of days and a boy of fourteen keeps a count of years and the two have never met.** **The eleventh line of the second column is the only thing in that city both of them can be the age of.** Held open, and it is the volume's smallest live object: **that a child of fourteen in this city is the only person who can be right about a number in that yard, and that a man of thirty-two with a limp wrote nine names of places in a notebook in a market in a quarter of an hour and cannot use any of them, and that the seven names and the eleventh line are four inches apart in the same pocket and have not met.**

14. **A man in a city four hundred miles off, a county, a bench of nine, a reach, a woman with a door on a lane, a second copyist and a yard on Weir Street, and all of them off the page for all ten chapters of this block.** **The breach is open and is not repaired and no apology in any form was available to anybody and none was made; Mara Vey is not in this block and her sentence is not re-opened, re-told, shortened or improved on, and nobody asks him about the morning and he does not offer it; the not askings stand at nine and this block entered no tenth; the brothers are not healed and the word brother is not used in a room the two of them were in, because they are not in a room together; and Toban's yard is off the page for thirty chapters of this volume, which is the volume's second-largest cost after the city, and it is recorded rather than passed over.** Held open for Block 4 and Block 5.

15. **A defect of this project's own record, and the eighth worked example of it, and the second in two blocks that a parity check did not see.** **A scripted bounded device pass over ten files took `about` from seven hundred and nine to three hundred and eighty and `about N seconds` from sixteen to zero, and reading every changed line afterwards, as continuity items 561 and 584 require, found fourteen defects, and every one of the fourteen was found by reading a file and not by counting anything: two broken sentences, nine half-substituted phrases of the city's own figure, a swallowed figure in a chapter's opening, sixteen stripped hedges, seven stripped hedges off a canon figure, an italic span opened and never closed, a closing bold marker with no opening marker, a half-completed substitution, a question-count inconsistency between two chapters, seven main-world weekdays in a city scene, and three narrator's leaks of this book's own structure onto a page.** **Three of the fourteen were bold-span defects and one of those passed a per-line parity check because the span was opened by hand and the line simply ended, which is the second time in two blocks that reading a rendered line and not counting asterisks is the only thing that finds it.** A per-line bold parity check across all two hundred and thirty chapter files returns zero, a check for a bold span left open returns zero, a duplicate-paragraph sweep across the ten new chapters returns clean, and a sweep for the eleven main-world month names, the twelve real-world month names and the seven weekday names returns zero, **and all four were run here after every repair and are recorded as having been run, which is the seventh consecutive block in which the first three return zero.**

16. **And the standing debt, untouched and carried forward an eighth time.** **`outline/series.md`'s Volume 5 escalation line is not satisfied in full and this block is not to be read as satisfying it; the pressure arrived without a member, in a printed form with four spaces for a figure and nowhere at all for a name, in a market, and in a man of forty-six with a satchel who is the most ordinary person in the volume, and the first named member of the Order on a page is Volume 06's. The antagonist's name is not written anywhere in Chapters 221 to 230, the separate-sheet rule holds, and thread 89 and the Volume 02 close's §7.1 still hold the second severance for Chapter 894.** Nothing in this block joined any two halves of anything and nothing in it is going to.

17. **What the Batch 0003 review repair pass changed, and the ninth worked example of the same defect, and the first one a whole block's narration was at fault for.** **The prose phase's own repair pass found fourteen defects of its own text by reading, and the review of commit `980a374` then found twelve more, eight of them fact defects on the page and four of them false statements in this project's own state files, and the state-file class is the one that matters most, because a downstream writer inherits every one of them as fact.** **What is now corrected and what a Batch 0004 writer must not re-derive: the page carries the eleventh line's age and the absent outcome column on the twenty-sixth and not the twenty-fifth, and he was given the name of one of the eleven nine times over and not the names of nine of them; the gate's four words are four words; the glass man's ascents and the duckboard's five days on the twenty-eighth and seven on the thirtieth are the figures on the page; the two men the woman of fifty spoke to are about nineteen days apart; the page in the coat was last looked at on the twenty-third; the lineage reading gives the shape of a decision and not the reason for it; and the city's figure is hedged in all twenty-three of its occurrences.** **What the state files had wrong and did not: the word *brother* was recorded as unused in this block and it is used once, in Chapter 228, by a man of fifty-two about a brother of his own at the low end of the river who thinks he is on a roof, and it is in his own mouth as the reason his first of two things is believable; `two` was 330 and is 329; and the reporting clause was 158 in 32,103 words and is 35 in 30,038, kept only where the city's oral habit is the point and named at continuity item 603. None of the sixteen threads above is closed by any of this and none may be closed by Batch 0004.**

## Threads Volume 05 Batch 0004 carries into Batch 0005

**The full record is `state/continuity.md` items 604 to 618, the Relationship state at the end of Chapter 240, the `## Batch 0004 — Volume 05, Chapters 231–240, "The Road That Is Not Made"` entry in `state/batch-summaries.md`, and the per-chapter entries and Block 4 note in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. This section is a reading list, not a substitute. Nothing below is closed, nothing below is answered, and nothing below was answered by the Volume 04 close or by Volume 05 Batch 0001 or Batch 0002 or Batch 0003 or by this block.** **The seventeen threads Batch 0003 carried into Batch 0004 all stand exactly as they stood, except thread 6, which this block touched and did not close, and this block opened twelve of its own. The section is seventeen items and the seventeenth is the ninth worked example of this project's own process defect, added because a later writer inheriting this block has to know the block was rewritten rather than repaired.** Batch 0005 writes Chapters 241 to 250, which is Block 5 and is the volume's close, and its own question is thread 2 below.

1. **The message system, and a strip on a shelf with a mark in the corner of it that nobody in that city put there.** A strip of paper about a foot long, ruled in five lines by the same rule nine inches long and the same strip of whalebone that make the city's forms, with nothing written on any of the five and a mark put in the corner in a child's left hand, corded into bales of cord and reed and carried out of a gate four days in seven by nine people who are not told what is in a bundle. **One came back on the thirty-fifth day of a count of days that this city keeps, with the cord neither cut nor retied, and it went onto the shelf with the other eight of its kind and it was not counted.** Held open: **where it came from, whether another has come back since, what the mark on it is, and that four people looked at it for four minutes and not one of them said anything and nobody in this block explained it and nobody in this block may.** The system has no name in the main world and no office and the block gave it none.

2. **The volume's remaining chain and Block 0005's own question, which is what the main world does with a refusal.** **The city is behind the reader at the fortieth day of a count of ninety that the city keeps and the main world cannot name, and nothing of it is on the page, and a strip is on a shelf in it and a man of fifty-two is on a ladder four times a day on a wall that is four hundred years old.** **A printed sheet of the office's series is about four days off a town two hundred miles off with a place name and the word *eastern* in the object column and four empty places in it, carried by a man of about thirty-one who has left about two thousand sheets at about two thousand doors in six years, and about four hundred doors on his list and about four hundred doors on another list in the county four hundred miles off are the same four hundred doors and nobody in either county has ever worked out how many.** Block 0005 is *What the Main World Does With a Refusal*: the office reads the refusal in a file and not in a room, a figure of persons against a district, a bench of nine asked a seventh thing and saying no in eleven words, a market Saturday, and a last image that is not a door. **And the block must not read a document in a file as a scene, and must not have a character in a room with anybody who realises anything, and the ninth refusal in four hundred years is a nine-word form on a printed sheet and the count of it is a count of refusals and is not a figure of persons and the two may never be added.**

3. **The refusal arithmetic, which now has four numbers and not three, and which nobody has compared.** A book under a board in a bindle yard has **nine** lines, the ninth made on the thirty-ninth day of a count of days that this city keeps, and the count went up for the first time in a count of days anybody in the city is keeping. A man of sixty-six's head says **eleven**, and he said so out loud in a yard in about nineteen words and **has not added the two and says he is not going to.** A sheet that went out of the city on the thirty-ninth of this count went out with **four empty places** in it, and that is the first time in a count of days any man in that city can remember that a sheet of that series has gone back out with nothing in the four places. **The eight, the nine and the ten of Batch 0003 and the nine and the eleven of this block are five numbers on five pieces of paper in five places and nobody in this city has put any two of them beside any other.** Held open: **what the office does with a sheet that comes back with four empty places in it, and whether a room two hundred miles off has both returns from one district in a pile, and the man of forty-six's prediction, that nobody in a room adds two figures from one district together and nobody asks why they are different, which is on the page in Chapter 229 and is not a narrator's verdict and is not contradicted by anything.**

4. **The word *eastern* on a printed sheet, and a man of thirty-two who has read it before and has not said so.** It is in a sheet of good paper in a bag in the ordinary post in a county four hundred miles off, in a copy on a shelf in that county, and now in the object column of a sheet of the office's series, in front of the name of a place a man of sixty-six wrote on the twenty-third day. **Nobody in that city asked what it means, and a woman of sixty read it off a sheet in a long room and did not ask, and a clerk of forty entered two lines on the strength of it and does not read them back, and a boy of fourteen said the only true thing anybody said about the six columns, which is that it wants people and it will not say people.** Held open: **that the office filled the object column in and the man who printed it never saw the word, and that no character in this volume may join any two of the four documents, and that the district the office has now got is one the office has never been told exists.**

5. **A figure of persons, a drawer, and a second answer that was in a room for an hour and nobody knew.** The only figure of persons this city can put on a piece of paper is one already written down, and it is the list of the third shift in a drawer, with a man of fifty-two's name on it and the names of the two men before him, and about a few hundreds of people in that city could be asked what the third shift is and not one of them would be able to say. **Nobody said it out loud in that yard and the man of thirty-two worked it out at a yard's mouth and has not written it down.** And the second answer, which is that to put a number of persons against an unnamed object somebody must put a name to the object, and one man in that city can do that with a hand, and he was standing at the mouth of a yard with a stick, **and the second answer is the power he has, and on the fortieth day he did not use it, and nobody in the city saw him not use it.** Held open: **the drawer, the roster, the third of the six, and the fact that the line at the top of the ninth refusal's page is the right size and the wrong shape and the man of sixty-six has not written in it and is not going to.**

6. **A man of fifty-two, the third shift, and nineteen years of a ladder, and the finding he said out loud on the thirtieth day of Batch 0003.** **This block touched him twice and both times it is a thing he does and neither is a second account of him: he stood at the end of a bench with his hands behind his back for about nine minutes and did not do a thing wrong and did not do a thing right, and he came down the ladder on the fortieth day a minute early and stood at the bottom of it, and nobody in that yard asked him why and about four hundred people in a city of about nine thousand people did not know that the man on the third shift stood at the bottom of a ladder on the fortieth day of a count of days this city keeps.** He has said nothing in this block; the two things and the third thing are in Chapters 228 and 230 and he has not said any of them again. **The thread is not closed: the roster itself is not on the page, the drawer is not open, the third of the six is not described, and the city's finding — that it has begun filing a person as a class — was said on the thirtieth day and has not been acted on and will not be.**

7. **A man of sixty-one, a fold, and a road offered and refused.** He refused a column in nineteen words in a doorway on the thirty-first day, stood in a room at the top of a lane on the fortieth day for the first time in nineteen years eleven feet from a man of sixty-six, **said the mechanic's other half once and in his own words, stood up and offered a man of thirty-two a fourth route on terms with nothing in them, and said he would not be the first of the eleven hands in four hundred years to be written in a hand and would not let a man of thirty-two be one either.** **The offer is a document in a book under a board and is not going anywhere and the man is not going to take it, and the refusal is four sheets in the same register as the Volume 04 refusal and in a different sentence, and the difference is on the page and is that this time he was asked by two people in a room and not on a sheet in good paper.** Held open: **the eleven roads in the second column, the first of the eleven which was never read on the page, the fourteenth-year-old line with two marks on it and no outcome column, and the fact that the two books have a fold in the same place and neither man has ever said so and neither has compared a figure.**

8. **The lineage reading, made once, in Chapter 227, and the not making of it on the fortieth day.** **The block staged the decline and the reading is still one reading in the volume: on the fortieth day, at a lane that goes up and not down, with about two hundred and forty pieces of paper on a table, his left hand was warm and he had been going to do a thing and did not do it, and it is the same thing and the same hand and the same warmth, and the consequence on the page is that he never finds out what the word on the sheet means.** The eleven lines are referred to twice in this block and are not re-derived; the eleventh of them is a man of fifty-two on a shift nineteen years old and he cannot stop seeing it in a city of about nine thousand people and he cannot stop seeing it in a town of about four thousand. Held open: **the residue, which is a line he wrote on the seventeenth day of Hearthmonth and did not write a figure under, and two unreconciled numbers on a page in a coat, and the panel, which is one of five in the block and of which he has written none down.**
