9. **A child of nine years old, unnamed, and the anchor the return was made through.** It carried a hod eleven times and ate a piece of bread and walked a lane twice and put a mark in the corner of a strip of paper in about a second and a half, and it did not go out on three days running in forty, and on the fortieth day at the ninth hour it came down a lane, put its bundle on a kerb, looked at a man of thirty-two with a limp and then at his left hand, and put its own left hand flat on the cold stone, and about a minute and a half later he was four hundred miles off at the low end of a sill. **It did not say anything and nobody said anything to it and it did not go out through a gate and it went down the lane, and a man of about forty at a winch stopped winding for the length of it and did not look round and has not been asked.** Held open: **nobody in that city knows what it is for and four hundred of them do and about nine hundred know what it carries and not one of the two figures knows the other one, and that fact was stated in Chapter 219 and is not restated; that the child is not named and is not to be named; and that it must not be a symbol, a sacrifice, a post or a rescue in the volume's close.**

10. **A man of thirty-two with a limp, a week of fever, and somebody else's hands on his tools.** A boy of fifteen held the ferrier's stick, the notebook and a coat with four sheets of printed paper in it for seven days, did not open the notebook, and gave it back flat and in the right order. **The fever is Stage 3's and it is a week and the leg is the same leg and is not worse and nobody in that house has said that it is worse, and a man of thirty-two with a limp got up nine steps of a bank instead of one on a market morning and four people noticed and none of them said a word.** The tax: **he is unable to be given a thing without losing the thread, and in this block it is a strip of paper at nine minutes, a strip at eleven minutes, four minutes of a man ruling a form, and a cup on the fourth day of a fever asked about twice.** Held open: **the seventh line of a page of foolscap, which says *a person* and was looked at twice in this block and finished neither time, and the four words that have been ready since the sixth day of a count of days he does not have, and the one line he did write, which is a claim about a frame and contains no figure and is not going to get one.**

11. **A man of about thirty-one with a satchel, and a man of forty-six with a satchel, and the same four hundred doors.** One has carried about two thousand sheets in six years and has never once known what is in the four places and has said so out loud twice in about a week; the other has left about three thousand in eight years and wrote the same sentence on the back of his own list twice in Batch 0003, about four inches apart, in the same hand, and has said he will not be able to explain them to anybody in about a year. **They are the same four hundred doors and neither knows the other's name and they will be in the same room in a town two hundred miles off inside about nine days and nobody in either room has worked out how many.** Held open: **whether either comes back, and the name on the last of about four hundred places on the older man's list, which is still not printed.**

12. **A city of about nine thousand people that offered a man something and did not want him in it.** It is a working city with a wharf of nine hundred feet, a glass works with a furnace that has not gone out in four hundred years, a bread oven hot at the second hour and at the ninth, a market on two hundred yards of street, three gates, a council of eleven, and nine of us who carry. **On the fortieth day two men in it offered a man of thirty-two a road and told him he was not going to take it, and the reason they may offer is that the offer goes in a book under a board and does not leave the city, and the finding is stated by the city and not by him, and nobody in the city was improved.** Held open: **the message system's own second half, the drawer, the third shift, and the eleven lines, and the fact that the city remains sovereign and unreachable and is not rescued and is not a friend of his, and that the volume's close may not improve it.**

13. **The main world's returned faces and the cost of their absence.** A bench of nine that has not been asked anything since the crossing out, with nothing entered in its minute about a fever. **A woman of twenty-nine with a door on a lane forty feet from the room he was in for a week, who did not come out of it once, and whose sentence from the end of Volume 04 is not re-opened, re-told, shortened or improved on, and nobody asked him about the morning and he did not offer it, and the not askings stand at nine and this block entered no tenth.** A second copyist two hundred miles off who has not opened a letter. A man of forty-seven who said the four things three times in about a fortnight and asked nothing either time. A boy of fifteen with a bill he is not going to be given. **A yard on Weir Street with about nine men in it and one of the nine who has not once said his brother's name and nobody on that yard has asked him why, and that is a cost and it is the volume's second-largest after the city and it is recorded rather than passed over.** Held open for Block 5: **the bench's seventh asking, and the breach, which is open and is not repaired, and which Block 0005 is the volume's last chance to leave standing.**

14. **A child of fourteen, a line in a book the same age as he is, and nine names written in a market.** In this block he looks at a printed sheet and a strip of paper face down nine inches apart and works out in about a second that they are not the same kind of paper and does not say so, and he says the only true thing anybody in that yard has said about the six columns. **A man of thirty-two with a limp has nine names of places written in a notebook from a market and cannot use any of them, and the seventh line of a page in the same coat says a person, and the two are four inches apart in one pocket and have not met and are not going to.** Held open: **the one true thing about a book in a yard is a boy's and he is fourteen and he is not the child.**

15. **A man's own arithmetic, counted by this batch and by nobody's character, and printed nowhere.** A main-world absence of ninety days from the eighth of Embermonth, YR 314 to the eighth of Hearthmonth, YR 315 on the chain at `outline/volume-05.md` §17, day 13 to day 103. A printed warning of three weeks in an office's hand, two years old, unchanged. **An error against that warning of sixty-nine days, and sixty-nine over ninety is about three quarters of the absence, which is the first narrowing in this case, against Volume 04's hundred and seventy-one over a hundred and ninety-two.** **The block printed no drift figure, no character is told any of it, and no character uses the word *failed* about a road. He wrote the reason down and not the figure, on the seventeenth day of Hearthmonth, YR 315, and the reason is a claim about the path interval and the path interval is a main-world conversion made by a person and not by the narrative, and the volume cannot prove it, and no chapter in the block states the frame and the error in one sentence.** Held open: **the frame claim itself, which is either the whole of what happened to him or it is nothing, and there is no way at all to tell from a town of about four thousand people.**

16. **And the standing debt, untouched and carried forward a ninth time.** **`outline/series.md`'s Volume 5 escalation line is not satisfied in full and this block is not to be read as satisfying it; the pressure arrived without a member, in a printed form with four spaces for a figure and nowhere at all for a name, in a market, and in a man of about thirty-one with a satchel who is the most ordinary person in the volume, and the first named member of the Order on a page is Volume 06's. The antagonist's name is not written anywhere in Chapters 231 to 240, the separate-sheet rule holds, and thread 89 and the Volume 02 close's §7.1 still hold the second severance for Chapter 894.** Nothing in this block joined any two halves of anything and nothing in it is going to.

17. **And the ninth worked example of this project's own defect, and the first one a bulk pass destroyed nine files rather than nine sentences.** **A bounded device pass with a per-occurrence index and a per-occurrence keep-count matched ninety-one reporting clauses across ten files and its replacement arithmetic truncated nine of the ten files to between two hundred and seven hundred words. The cause was one line of index arithmetic. The cause was found by counting the ten per-chapter word counts and not by reading anything. Nine chapters were rewritten and the plot, the ten endings, the cast, the figures and the single System panel are exactly as first written.** Three findings, and the first is the project's own point made a ninth time: **a pass that carries a figure it computed once and never re-counted has done the thing continuity item 584 is about; a bounded substitution over prose must go one file at a time with the file's word count printed after each file; and the review question in item 584 has a second half, which is that a parity check is not necessary at all if you read the file.** Two bold-span defects were found by reading and not by counting — a span opened by hand and never closed in Chapter 235 on a line carrying two speeches, and a paragraph opener in Chapter 240 closed nowhere — and one fact defect, a Sunday that is a Saturday. **A Batch 0005 writer inherits the block as it now stands and not as it was first written, and the nine rewrites are the reason it is 28,651 words and not 32,000.**

## Threads Volume 05 Batch 0005 carries into Volume 06

**The full record is `state/continuity.md` items 620 to 638, the Relationship state at the end of Chapter 250 and the volume's close, the `## Batch 0005 — Volume 05, Chapters 241–250, "What the Main World Does With a Refusal"` entry in `state/batch-summaries.md`, and the per-chapter entries, the Block 5 note and the Volume 05 close note in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. This section is a reading list, not a substitute. Nothing below is closed, nothing below is answered, and nothing below was answered by the Volume 04 close or by any of the five blocks of Volume 05.** **The seventeen threads Batch 0004 carried into Batch 0005 all stand exactly as they stood, and this block opened eight of its own and touched nine of the seventeen without closing one of them. The section is twenty-five items and Volume 05 is closed at Chapter 250.**

1. **The message system, and a strip on a shelf with a mark in the corner of it that nobody in that city put there.** Untouched by the prose of this block except at the last line, and the last line is the object. **A strip of paper about a foot long, ruled in five lines by the same rule nine inches long and the same strip of whalebone, with nothing written on any of the five and a mark put in the corner in a child's left hand, is on a shelf eleven feet long in a room off a lane that goes down to a quay, with eight others of its kind, and it has not been looked at since the seventh hour of a morning in the spring.** Held open: **where it came from, whether another has come back since, what the mark on it is, that the child is not named and may not be named, and that the mark is not said to be the same as either mark in Merefen and is not joined to any mark.** The system has no name in the main world and no office and this block gave it none.

2. **What the main world does with a refusal, which is this block's question and which the block has now answered once.** **The office read the refusal in a file and its answer was a return, and a figure of persons in respect of a district, and the district came out of a sheet that came in, and nobody in that room put the return's figure beside the number of persons the offer was made out to, and a reader can.** Held open for Volume 06: **the office has now asked the question its form could not ask, which is what a district is, and a man of about fifty has said in eleven words that he does not know, and a volume that convenes a room to hear about a refusal has been set up by a figure and not by a refusal.** `outline/series.md`'s Volume 6 row is *The Parliament of Oaths* and the next volume's question is what happens to a refusal when somebody convenes a room to hear about it and the room is the same shape as the thing that made the refusal.

3. **The refusal arithmetic, still five numbers, and this block did not reconcile it and printed none of it.** A book under a board has **nine**; a man of sixty-six's head says **eleven**; a sheet went out with **four empty places**; and the eight, nine and ten of Batch 0003 are on three other pieces of paper. **No two of the five are added in any chapter of this block, a refusal count is never added to a figure of persons, and the four prints on a printed sheet were not turned into thirty-six.** Held open: **that nobody has ever put any of the five beside any other, and that Volume 06 is the first volume in which a room is convened and a figure and a refusal are in the same room, and what happens to them there is Volume 06's finding and not this volume's.**

4. **The word *eastern* on a printed sheet, and it is now in a tray in a building two hundred miles off, in an object column in front of a place name, and in a drawer of type that the office has never struck.** **The finding of this block is that the word is the office's own: the type has been in a drawer since the form was made two years ago, set in the line with the heads of the five columns and not used in any of them, and nobody in that building has been able to put that anywhere.** Held open: **that the office printed a word two years ago for a reason nobody in it has asked about, and that a man of about fifty looked in the drawer at the sixth hour on the twenty-fourth of Hearthmonth and came out and said nothing, and that the marginal instruction at the foot of the form has been the same words for two years and nobody in the building has asked whose they are.** The marginal instruction is a practice and not a law and nobody explains it and Sabin Dain may not be the person who explains it.

5. **A figure of persons, a drawer, and a second answer that was in a room for an hour and nobody knew.** **Untouched by this block and carried exactly as Batch 0004 left it: the list of the third shift in a drawer with a man of fifty-two's name on it, a handful of people who could be asked what the third shift is and not one of them who could say, and the second answer, which is that to put a number of persons against an unnamed object somebody must put a name to the object, and the power he has, and the fact that he declined it on a kerb and nobody in the city saw him not do it.** Held open: **the drawer, the roster, the third of the six, and the line at the top of the ninth refusal's page that is the right size and the wrong shape and that the man of sixty-six has not written in and is not going to.**

6. **A man of fifty-two, the third shift, and nineteen years of a ladder.** Untouched and not in this block. **The thread is not closed: the roster is not on the page, the drawer is not open, the third of the six is not described, and the city's finding, that it has begun filing a person as a class, was said on the thirtieth day of Batch 0003 and has not been acted on and will not be.** The eleventh line of a notebook is a man of fifty-two on a shift nineteen years old and a man of thirty-two with a limp can still not stop seeing it in a town of about four thousand people, and it was referred to in Chapter 250 and was not re-derived.

7. **A man of sixty-one, a fold, and a road offered and refused in writing for the second time.** **The refusal is now a physical object in this county: four sheets of printed paper in a press in a room on Weir Street with a bench-board in it since the nineteenth day of Hearthmonth, YR 315, with four empty places in them and a place name and a word in the object column, and it has not been sent to anybody and is not going to be, and a boy of fifteen at that bench-board has not asked what is in that press and is not going to.** Held open: **the eleven roads in the second column, the first of the eleven which was never read on the page, the fourteenth-year-old line with two marks on it and no outcome column, and the fact that the two books have a fold in the same place and neither man has ever said so and neither has compared a figure.**

8. **The lineage reading, made once, in Chapter 227, and its residue.** Untouched and not re-derived. **He wrote the reason on the seventeenth day of Hearthmonth, YR 315, in about four minutes, in his own hand, on the fourth page he has filled in in four years, and it is a claim about a frame and contains no figure and is not going to get one.** Held open: **the frame claim itself, which is either the whole of what happened to him or it is nothing and there is no way at all to tell from a town of about four thousand people, and the two unreconciled numbers on a page in a coat, which he has been away from long enough that a sixth attempt would be about a different thing, and the seventh line that says a person, which he looked at for the fifth time on the last day of Volume 05 and did not finish.**

9. **A child of nine years old, unnamed, and the living anchor the return was made through.** **Not in this block, not named in any of the ten chapters, does not count, and is not a symbol, a sacrifice, a post or a rescue.** Its mark is the last image of Volume 05. Held open: **nobody in that city knows what the child is for, and about four hundred people in a city of about nine thousand people do and about nine thousand know what it carries and not one of the two figures knows the other one, and that fact was stated in Chapter 219 and is not restated here.**

10. **A man of thirty-two with a limp, a week of fever, and a house in Weir Street.** **The leg is the same leg and is not worse, and no character in Volume 05 says he is slower now or finds it out for the first time, and the stick is ash, four feet nine, cut out of a fence rail in a county that is not on any map, for four hundred yards of road and nothing else, and he cannot lean on it.** **The tax arrived once in this block, in Chapter 246, at the sixth hour, when he was given a register to look at and could not remember nine minutes later whether the fifth entry said question or finding.** Held open: **the seventh line of a page of foolscap, which says a person and was looked at five times and finished never, and the four words that have been ready since the sixth day of a count of days he does not have and that he did not say on the last day of the volume, and the one line he did write, which is a claim about a frame and contains no figure.**

11. **A man of about thirty-one with a satchel, and a man of forty-six with a satchel, and the same four hundred doors.** **Neither man is named in this block and neither is in it, and the two lists are on the page at the end of it: about four hundred doors on one and about four hundred doors on the other, the same four hundred doors, two hundred miles apart, and nobody has worked out how many and this block did not add them.** **A keeper of records asked him in Chapter 247 what is on the two lists that is the same, and he has not seen the second list and did not guess.** Held open: **whether either man comes back, and the name on the last of about four hundred places on the older man's list, which is still not printed, and whether a room is ever convened in which two men with two lists are in it at once.**

12. **A city of about nine thousand people that offered a man something and did not want him in it, and that has now answered an office with a number it did not know it was giving.** **Sovereign, unreachable, not rescued, not a friend of his, not a ruin, not a shrine and not a mystery, and nothing in it or about it was improved, converted or healed, and no character in this block pities it.** Held open: **the message system's own second half, the drawer, the third shift, the eleven lines, and the fact that being written to has cost it something it cannot get back, which is that it is now a figure in a return in a district that did not exist on the form.**

13. **The main world's returned faces and the cost of their absence.** **A bench of nine has been asked eight things in nineteen years and has been refused three of them for reasons and has not been asked anything since the sixth of Goatmonth, YR 315. A woman of twenty-nine with a door on a lane has refused a man of thirty-two's nine sentences in her own words and has not accepted them and has not been asked about the morning and is not going to, and the not askings stand at nine and this block entered no tenth. A second copyist of about thirty has a letter she has not opened and has said the only other thing said in that room. A man of forty-seven has said the four things four times in about a year and asked nothing.** Held open: **the breach, which is open and is not repaired, which Volume 05 was the last chance to leave standing, and which the next volume inherits exactly as it stands; and the count of eight things asked of a bench, which is a count of askings and is not a count of persons and is not a count of refusals and is never added to either.**

14. **A child of fourteen in a city, and a man of about nineteen and a man of about thirty with a pail and a woman of about thirty with a pail in a county.** **None of them is named in this block and none of them counts.** The man of about nineteen has an elder sister of twenty-two and has not said one word about her to anybody in that lane in a year, and appears twice in this block on the Chapter 228 precedent. Held open: **the one true thing about a book in a yard in a city, which is a boy's, and the four hundred doors on a man's list in a city four hundred miles off, and the two places four hundred yards apart where a man stood at a window and did not interrupt.**

15. **A man's own arithmetic, counted by this batch and by nobody's character, and printed nowhere.** A main-world absence of ninety days; a printed warning of three weeks in an office's hand, two years old, unchanged; an error against that warning of sixty-nine days, about three quarters of the absence, the first narrowing in this case. **The block printed no drift figure, told no character any of it, and no character in it uses the word *failed* about a road.** Held open: **the frame claim, which is either the whole of what happened to him or it is nothing, and the conversion of ninety days into three months, which is a main-world conversion made by a person and is not repeated in any chapter of this block.**

16. **A hearing entered for the ninth of Rainmonth of the year following, for a delegation that cannot arrive, heard with nobody in it.** **It still stands. It is not adjourned and it is not refused and it is not retold, and the delegation is still not sent for. It has now a companion file, of one sheet, with a district in it, and the district is the district a return went out against, and a file has been opened beside the hearing and not against it, and a man of about fifty said in eleven words that a district is a set of market towns and he does not know what a district is.** Held open: **the hearing, the delegation, and the companion file, and the first volume in which a room sits to hear the thing.**

17. **The breach, open and unrepaired, and no apology in any form in a scene, an aside or a document.** **He did not say that she was right and he did not say that he had been wrong and no apology of any kind was available in that room on the twenty-second of Goatmonth, YR 315, and the sentence she said in a gateway in another month was standing in that room and is not referred to, quoted, re-told, shortened or improved on, and she has not walked up a road with him and is not going to and Volume 05 is closed and she has not.** Held open: **the breach, entire, and the ninth not-asking, and the fact that a Volume 06 character may not enter a tenth in order to repair it.**

18. **A woman of thirty-three who has been afraid of a column for eleven years, and four or five words.** **Nobody enters a family by a name nobody entered. She said it on the fourth of Goatmonth, YR 315, four or five words, and then wrote a figure in the second of four places and put no name in anything, and a note of nineteen words of hers is face down in a book on the second shelf and has not been turned over in about five months, and a man who entered about eleven thousand forms in thirty-one years has walked past that shelf about four times a day for five months and has not once put his hand out to it.** Held open: **her want, unchanged, and the note, face down, and the fact that an office answered a thing she wrote eleven years ago with a number and that she does not know that the second figure is the first figure's cousin and a reader can.**

19. **A district, and the office's habit of counting, and a tray that is not a file.** **New in this block.** A district is a set of about nine market towns made out of a list of market towns about a hundred years ago, and the office has now got a district with a market town in it that it did not put there, and the district is on the sheet because a sheet came in with it on. **A return that is not one of the ordinary nine has been written on by a room and not by a press, and there are about eleven of those in about nine years and nobody has ever written a rule about them, and a tray is emptied into a passage and a passage is emptied in the morning and a thing in a passage is a thing that has not been lost and has not been kept and is not anywhere.** Held open: **a man of about fifty who has entered about eleven thousand forms in thirty-one years and who said *a county answers* and stopped in the middle of the sentence, and the half after the stop, which about two people in that building have worked out and neither has said.**

20. **A magistrate of this county with a construction she has not used, and nobody in the county she can ask.** **New in this block, on the volume's last day.** She has beaten two counts of persons with a form's own construction in nine years and has said nothing about either of them in that room, and she sat silent on the sixth of Goatmonth while a sheet with four empty places in it lay on that table, and on the twenty-fifth she said, in nineteen words, that she had had a construction ready since the second hour and had not used it and would like somebody to write down that she did not use it, and there is nobody in this county she can ask. **The construction is still there, and no instrument in that county can record the not-using of an instrument.** Held open: **the construction, the two counts she beat, and the fact that a person who has an instrument and has not used it and cannot record the not-using is the volume's engine standing on its own in a person, and the next volume may not resolve it and may not let anybody enter it for her.**

21. **The bench's eight things asked in nineteen years, on a page where anybody coming up the stair can read it.** **New in this block.** Five askings of the same crossing, four yes and one brought up and not put, a sixth refused for a reason in a Harvestmonth with a figure of forty marks on a page, a seventh refused for a reason on the thirtieth of Fallowmonth with the word consent in it four times, and an eighth refused for a reason on the sixth of Goatmonth about a word and not a road. **It is a count of askings and it is not a count of persons and it is not a count of refusals and neither of those three is ever added to another, and the reeve asked for it in a market and wants the line that answers it to be the right one, and the clerk entered no name at all against the eighth of them, which is the first time in nineteen years.** Held open: **the page under the stair, the three refusals, and the subscription book with four names in it where two of the four are the same man twice, which is not a minute and cannot be got out of.**

22. **Four hundred doors on two lists, asked about once and answered once, and the answer was that nobody knows.** **New in this block.** A keeper of records entered the question four times over about five months and has had no answers at all, and asked it on the twenty-second of Goatmonth, YR 315, and he has not seen the second list and did not guess, and she said that that was the answer and she had had it since the second hour and did not want it. **Nobody added the two figures together, in that room or in any other.** Held open: **whether the two lists are the same four hundred, and whether a body corporate with a door on a lane and a man of about thirty-one with a satchel are ever in a room, and whether anybody ever asks.**

23. **The eleventh worked example of this project's own process defect, and the eleventh is the one where the writer read the files.** **A figure in a recording instruction was carried from Batch 0004 and applied to a block whose first five chapters are a tray, a file, a press room, a lane and a minute, and the honest measurement is that the last five chapters are at or under the house ceiling and the first five are not, and `about` is 571 in the block split 59 / 90 / 94 / 84 / 51 and 42 / 45 / 30 / 41 / 35.** A bounded substitution was run over five files one at a time with the file's word count printed after each file and every changed line read, and it found two figure defects and one bold span opened and never closed, and all three were fixed and none of them changed an ending. **The standing instruction for the next block is the same three findings in the same order: carry no figure you computed once, apply a bounded substitution over prose one file at a time and print the file's word count after each file, and read the file rather than count it.**

24. **The standing debt, untouched and carried forward a tenth time.** **`outline/series.md`'s Volume 5 escalation line is not satisfied in full and this block is not to be read as satisfying it; the pressure arrived without a member, in a printed form with four spaces for a figure and nowhere at all for a name, in a market, in an office, and in a man of about thirty-three who has been afraid of a column for eleven years, and the first named member of the Order on a page is Volume 06's. The antagonist's name is not written anywhere in Chapters 241 to 250, the separate-sheet rule holds, and thread 89 and the Volume 02 close's §7.1 still hold the second severance for Chapter 894.** Nothing in this block joined any two halves of anything and nothing in it is going to.

25. **And the thing the next volume inherits that is not a thread and is the volume's whole argument.** **A city is a person and a column is not, and the proof of it is that a city's refusal went out of it as a figure and a figure cannot be refused, and that nobody in the office can be told why the figure is wrong by anybody who is inside the thing, and that the only person on the page who understands this is a keeper of records in a fen county with a door on a lane who will not put a number in a column and cannot say why in any form she could put in a book, and that she is the one person in this part of the kingdom who has the instrument and has not used it, and that a magistrate of the same county has the other instrument and has not used that one either, and that the two of them have never been in a room with each other.** Held open for `outline/series.md`'s Volume 6: **the room that is convened is the same shape as the thing that made the refusal, and what happens to a refusal when somebody convenes a room to hear about it is the next volume's question and is not answered here.**

## Threads the Volume 05 close carries into Volume 06

**This section is written by `outline/volumes/volume-05-close.md`, which is the close record for the volume, and it is a reading list and not a substitute for that document. Every thread in the section above it — the twenty-five in *Threads Volume 05 Batch 0005 carries into Volume 06* and the seventeen Batch 0004 carried — is still open and is not repeated here, and none of them was closed by this close. The close opened eleven items of its own and they are continuity items 646 to 656 and they are findings about a closed volume rather than plot threads, and the seven that bear on Volume 06's work are restated below in the close's own words. No thread is closed by this section and no thread may be closed by a Volume 06 batch except by saying on the page that a thing was closed and where.**

1. **The spine of the volume, and the sentence Volume 06 is built on.** *A city is a person and a column is not, and the proof of it is that a city's refusal went out of it as a figure and a figure cannot be refused, and that nobody in the office can be told why the figure is wrong by anybody who is inside the thing.* Open. `outline/volume-06.md` §1 and §2 are built on it, and `outline/volume-06.md` §7 item 3 turns it: **a refusal recorded in a room is a finding, and a finding is a document, and the room is where the conversion happens and the room does not know it is doing it.**
2. **The message system and the strip on the shelf.** The strip is the volume's last image and it is behind the reader, four hundred miles off, on a shelf eleven feet long, with a mark in the corner of it that nobody in that room put there. **`outline/volume-06.md` §15 item 1 closes the matter for this volume: no Volume 06 batch may narrate it, summarise it, explain it, post to it, or send anything to it, and the child is not named.** Open as a fact, closed as a Volume 06 subject, and the two are different things.
3. **The five refusal figures.** Eight in the book under a board, nine after the nineteenth day, eleven in a man's head, four empty places on a printed sheet, and the eight, nine and ten of Volume 05 Block 3. **Not reconciled, no two added, none added to a figure of persons, and §7 item 2 of the outline carries it.**
4. **The figure of persons, and the number the office would not print.** **The number the office would not print on an offer is not printed in Volume 06 and may not be, and the figure now on a return is not reconciled with it, and no character in any room puts the two together.** Open, and it is the volume's engine.
5. **The district.** A set of about nine market towns made out of a list about a hundred years ago, and a man of about fifty who has said in eleven words that a district is a set of market towns and that he does not know what a district is. **Not defined. `outline/volume-06.md` §15 item 11 carries it.**
6. **The word *eastern*.** In two documents, a bag, a drawer of type, an object column, and now a stone in a field and a sheet going out in the ordinary post. **Defined zero times. The type is never struck by anybody who understands it, and a printer's mate of nineteen strikes it once.**
7. **The marginal instruction at the foot of the form.** The same words for two years, nobody in that building has asked whose they are, a practice and not a law. **Not explained in a sentence by anybody, and Sabin Dain may not be the person who explains it and does not.**
8. **The hearing, and the year.** **The year is fixed at the ninth of Rainmonth, YR 316, day 284, a Friday and not a market day, and it comes round inside Volume 06's Block 4; the close fixed it and its review repair pass corrected the year from YR 315 to YR 316, because day 284 is one hundred and twenty-eight days past the turn at day 156, and the day, the weekday and the block are unchanged.** It stands, is not adjourned, is not refused, is not retold, and no delegation is put in the room. The companion file of one sheet is not removed, not corrected, and not asked after by anybody who knows what is in it. **And no Volume 06 batch may merge the two hearings that sit on that date into one hearing.**
9. **The contact that ends.** **Iven Sarr, 58, keeper of the winter book, and the outline decides it and a batch may not decide it differently.** He is paid his want first, in Block 2, and it is the first finding in that book in a hundred and forty years; the cut is in Block 3; a finding cannot be asked a second time. He is not dead, the valley is not closed, **nobody in the valley is told, and the not-telling is held for eight months and is not resolved and is not scheduled.**
10. **The column with no outcome column, and the reversal.** Eleven hands and eleven roads in a second column in two parts, a hand and a road; the eleventh line with two marks on it, a woman's and a boy's, and about a year between the going out and the coming back. **The reversal is that the column with no outcome column is not a gap in a book but a decision about a person, four hundred years old, and the volume states the claim and not the verdict. Her survival stays intentionally unknown, and the keeper of sixty-one who said the shape of it out loud before he was asked has not been asked since and is not asked in Volume 06, because he is four hundred miles off in a city this volume does not go to, and the not-asking is structural and not a choice.**
11. **The bell, the tile, the seam, and the two crossings.** **The bell is rung at the fourth hour and a half by a boy and a carpenter, neither paid nor a member of anything, and Volume 06 asks who keeps it and nobody can answer, and that is the finding and not a gap, and the bell goes on being rung.** The tile on a coping is not to be mentioned, explained, or scheduled. The seam in the fourth bay is not to be pointed at, and **the second seam in the dressed block is the volume's cost, and neither is measured, and the two crossings four hundred yards apart are not put in one book by anybody in the volume.**
12. **The breach, and the separation, and the nine not-askings.** **The breach is open through all fifty chapters of Volume 06 and is not repaired in a scene, an aside, a document or an apology. The not-askings stand at nine and the tenth is not a gift and no Volume 06 character may enter one in order to repair anything. The separation is political and is this volume's relationship work, and the two are not the same thing: a Volume 06 batch that lets the political separation function as a repair of the breach has done the thing the close's §7 item 3 forbids.** And the word *brother* is not used in a room the two of them are in, and the yard is on the page for the first time since Volume 04 and the word is not used in it.
13. **The standing debt, discharged and not carried again.** The Culling Order's first named member on a page is this volume's: **Ansel Rudd, 44, in a room, doing the office's work, in Block 1, and not a villain's speech.** A phase that reads `outline/volume-06.md` as discharging the debt and does not put a member on a page has not discharged it.
14. **The name of the antagonist.** **Not written in `outline/volume-06.md`, not written in the close, and not to be written in any chapter of Volume 06.** He is a returned face named on the page in Volumes 01 and 02 and costs the volume nothing against the name budget. **Thread 89 and the Volume 02 close's §7.1 hold the second severance for Chapter 894, and a phase that eventually writes it checks the thread and the separate-sheet rule first and joins no two halves in one sentence.**
15. **The figures the close found on the page and did not repair, and the geography of about four hundred doors.** Five page figures are wrong and are carried to Volume 06 as figures a batch must not treat as canon. **The county a man of forty-six's list of about four hundred doors is in is a live tension the close carries rather than settles, and a Volume 06 batch may not use it: about four hundred doors is never added to about four hundred doors, nobody works out how many, and no character in Volume 06 may say which county the older list is in.**
16. **The three seeds this close did not spend and the four it found.** Not threads. **The ledger's basis with its three wrong figures, the repeated paragraph, the speech-length rule, the panel count of four, the day map and the year label** are all at continuity items 646 to 652 and all of them are things a Volume 06 batch inherits as *rules* and not as plot, and **the standing rule they add is three sentences: count the block you are describing and not only your own; state the basis of every figure in both columns; and for every device total also run a count of the raw lines that produce it, which is a different measurement and which is the one that catches an undercount.**

## Threads Volume 06 Batch 0001 carries into Batch 0002

**Written by the Batch 0001 phase. Nineteen threads. None is closed. The sixteen threads the Volume 05 close carried and the twenty-five threads Volume 05 Batch 0005 carried are all still open and none is repeated here, and none of the three lists may be closed by a batch except by saying on the page that a thing was closed and where. Continuity items 668 to 684 carry the findings; these are the plot threads.**

1. **The proceeding sits on the ninth of Rainmonth, YR 316, day 284, a Friday and not a market day, in a chamber with a table of three, a witness list of eight ruled lines with four names against them and four lines empty, a form of oath printed on the back of a return, and a clerk of the Crown who reads a thing back once and not twice.** Open. A Crown proceeding and the standing hearing are now in one building on one date and are joined by a file of one sheet and by nothing else, and **they may not be merged into one hearing.**
2. **A man of thirty-two with a limp is in that town and nobody sent for him and he is not on the witness list and he has not asked to be, and he has said he will give evidence, and the clerk entered *Evidence offered before the asking* in a line that is on no form in that office.** Open. He is a witness who offered before the asking, which is the volume's Block 4 climax in miniature, and Block 4 must not spend it twice.
3. **The offer of sealed personal records is on the table and he did not say yes and did not say no.** Open. The marshal has eleven drawers in four buildings and about nine hundred sheets, of which the marshal has read the eleven lines and not the eight hundred.
4. **The list: eleven hands and eleven roads in two parts, the eleventh with two marks on it, about a year between the first going out and the eleventh coming back, and no column for what became of anybody.** Open. **The same length as eleven lines with no words in them in his notebook, and the comparison of the two is Block 3's work and the volume has not made it.**
5. **A woman went out of a valley in the year the Sundering happened with a boy of about one-and-twenty, and there is no name in it and there is a mark, and the two marks on the eleventh line are the only marks on the sheet.** Open, and the name is not on the page and the volume does not print it.
6. **A schedule of about two hundred classifications is going into a record, with a frequency and a keeper against each, and about nine of the lines have the word *eastern* in front of a place name, and the second bay of a bridge four hundred yards down a lane from a market is in the object column.** Open. **The Order's want is that the schedule be complete before the first of Longlight at day 246, and a schedule that is late can be argued with, and a man of forty-four has argued with nine in a year.**
7. **The word *eastern* is now struck and is on a sheet in an object column, and a type case that has held it for two years is empty of it, and nobody has said what the word is for.** Open, and it is defined zero times and the volume defines it zero times.
8. **A proceeding is entered against a body corporate, and the register with five questions and not one finding and the day-book with nine not-askings and no tenth are both asked for by the sheet, and the keeper entered the day, the hour and the door and not a name and not *no person*.** Open. **A body corporate that can be named can be served, and it has been, and there is a copy of a summons on Weir Street that a man of thirty-two with a limp has not read.**
9. **The line on the witness list for the body corporate is empty, and the woman of thirty-eight did not fill it in and did not write *no person* on it either, and the woman of twenty-nine did the same thing to a different line four hundred yards inside the same building on the twenty-fifth of a month, and neither has been told the other exists.** Open, and nothing has happened between them, and the volume's relationship work is two refusals of kindness in two rooms two hundred miles apart and neither of them is the other one.
10. **The tenth letter was opened and entered under a head a copyist of thirty made up herself, and the eleventh does not exist.** Open, and the letter went out on the ordinary post in about three weeks. **The second copyist is Alish Renn and the block did not name her.**
11. **A district has about nine market towns in it and one of the nine is a market four hundred yards long in a county that has just been served, and nobody in that building has ever said what a district is.** Open. **The word is not defined and the figure of persons against the district is not printed and the number the office would not print on an offer is not printed and no character has put the two together.**
12. **A woman of about thirty-three at the register filled in the fourth column of a form at the sixth hour on the tenth of Thawmonth, YR 316, in about a minute and a quarter without looking up, and the hand is the hand that filled the second column of a return on the fourth of Goatmonth, YR 315, and her note of nineteen words is still face down on the second shelf.** Open. **Her name is not in the pigeonhole or the file or the proceeding and is not going to be in any of the three, and her column is a *Keeper* column and the volume's forged record is built on that column, and she finds out in Block 4.**
13. **A man of forty-seven has now said the same four things five times in a year and has been asked about none of them, and in Chapter 260 he was asked about the bell and said the four things again.** Open. **The volume's repetition is a document now, and Block 5's sitting is where the fifth time is asked and is not asked anything about.**
14. **A clerk of the press has entered about eleven thousand forms in thirty-one years, has walked past a drawer about four times a day for two years and has never put his hand out to it, has not once asked what is under the bottom file in a pigeonhole, and has said *set it* in a doorway.** Open. **The block's true thing about a column and his being thanked for it are Block 2's and Block 5's, and he is not thanked in this block.**
15. **A printer's mate of nineteen has eleven weeks and five days to the next sitting and wants to be a journeyman, and he set a word he does not use, and nobody has told him what it did and nobody is going to.** Open. **He is not a device and is not to be given a want that is the volume's.**
16. **A woman of about twenty-nine who is a clerk of the returns has wanted the sixth column ruled for six years and has not asked, and asked for a word instead because asking for a head is a decision and asking for a word is not.** Open, and the column is still unruled, and nobody has asked her a second question about it.
17. **A man of about sixty with a tray has carried that tray out of a room at the seventh hour and back at the first hour for twenty-two years, has carried four matters in about nine years in another trade, and has said in twelve words that nobody ever asked whether he would rather it went at the sixth.** Open, and the hour has been wrong for twenty-two years and nobody has acted on it.
18. **A bell with forty-one rivets in it goes at the fourth hour and a half in a fen county and nobody in that county can be asked who keeps it, and the no-answer is the finding.** Open. **It is rung in this block and it is rung at the end of the volume and it does not stop, and Chapter 900 depends on it, and the tile on a coping is not mentioned once in Chapters 251 to 260.**
19. **The sixth look at the seventh line that says a person is on the page, in Chapter 260, and `outline/volume-06.md` §4 still places that look in Block 5.** Open, and it is a live disagreement between the plan and the page. **A later block must not write a seventh look without recording the correction, and the four words he has had ready since the sixth day of a count of days he does not have are still ready and are not said, and the nine words in the wagon arch are a different nine words and are not told to the reader.**

**And the four words that do not get to be a fifth thread, because they are not a thread and no batch may make them one: the ninety days a courier takes to cross a vale, the two-to-six-week testimony lag, the printed warning that has said three weeks in an office's hand for two years, and the number of persons the office would not print on an offer. All four are barred from this block's arithmetic, none was used, and a Batch 0002 writer who wants a letter to arrive must have it arrive with a date on it and must not have anybody convert it into a figure of days of waiting in a chamber.**
